\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j^{(1)})^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j^{(2)})^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(j^{(3)})^{\ast}}

The top row is obviously exact, and the bottom row is exact by
[
Mat22
]
(cf.
[
BS23
, Theorem 2.2]
). Observe that, by inspection,
(p,(E))
has height
2
in
\mathfrak{S}_{R}
, and thus by the flatness of
p_{k}^{(i)}
, the same holds for
(p,(E))
in
\mathfrak{S}^{(i)}
for
i=1,2,3
. Thus, we observe that the maps
(j^{(i)})^{\ast}
are fully faithful by Proposition
A.23
. Suppose now that
j^{(1)}_{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))}
is a vector bundle. Let us observe that

(j^{(i)})^{\ast}(p_{k}^{(i)})^{\ast}j^{(1)}_{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))}\simeq(p_{k}^{(i)})^{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))},

for each
i=1,2,3
and
k=1,\ldots,i
, again by Proposition
A.23
. Thus, by the fully faithfulness of the maps
(j^{(i)})^{\ast}
, we may pull back the descent data on
\mathcal{W}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))}
relative to the covering
\{p_{k}^{(2)}\colon U(\mathfrak{S}_{R}^{(2)},(E))\to U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))\}_{k=1,2}
to descent data on
j^{(1)}_{\ast}\mathcal{W}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))}
relative to the covering
\{p_{k}^{(2)}\colon\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R}^{(2)})\to\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R})\}_{k=1,2}
. This gives an object
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
whose image in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{W}
.

We have shown that anything satisfying the condition on the pushforward in the proposition statement is in the essential image of
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
. Conversely, if
\mathcal{W}
is in the essential image, then this pushforward condition holds again by applying Proposition
A.23
.
∎

1.3.
Tannakian theory

We now show the category of prismatic
F
-crystals (and quasi-syntomic
F
-torsors, see §
1.3.2
) possesses good Tannakian properties in the sense of Appendix
A
. We advise the reader to consult there for undefined terminology or notation, and to recall Notation
1.1
.

1.3.1.
Prismatic
F
-crystals

We begin by shortening some notation from §
A.5
:

\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\qquad\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)\vcentcolon=\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T).

Combining Proposition
1.17
and Theorem
A.19
, we see that
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
is reconstructible in
(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T,\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
, and every object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T,\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
is locally trivial.

Remark 1.27
.

Although our main interest is the case where
T=\ast
, to make sense of the notion of local triviality, it is natural to include the case of general sheaves
T
, at least that of the representable ones.

Set
\phi\colon\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\to\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
to be the morphism of group sheaves associating to every
(A,I)
the map
\mathcal{G}(A)\to\mathcal{G}(A)
obtained from
\phi_{A}\colon A\to A
. For an object
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
, denote by
\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{A}
the
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
-torsor
\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{A}
, in the notation of §
A.1
. Denote by
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]
the group sheaf
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]}
and let
\iota\colon\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\to\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]
be the obvious monomorphism. Finally, set
\mathcal{A}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]
to be
\iota_{\ast}\mathcal{A}
.

Definition 1.28
.

The category
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
of
prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure
over
T
has objects
(\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}})
where
\mathcal{A}
is an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
and
\varphi
is a
Frobenius isomorphism

\varphi_{\mathcal{A}}\colon(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{A})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{A}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}],

and morphisms are morphisms in
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
commuting with the Frobenii.

Objects of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
are pairs
(\omega,\varphi_{\omega})
with
\omega
an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T,\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
and
\varphi_{\omega}\colon(\phi^{\ast}\omega)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]
an isomorphism in
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{lt}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T,\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}])
. For an object
\mathcal{A}
in
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
there is a natural identification
\phi^{\ast}\omega_{\mathcal{A}}\simeq\omega_{\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{A}}
. So, for an object
(\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}})
of
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
there is a natural Frobenius
\varphi_{\omega_{\mathcal{A}}}
, and
(\omega_{\mathcal{A}},\varphi_{\omega_{\mathcal{A}}})
is an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
.

On the other hand, define
\mathbf{Twist}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}|_{T}}^{\varphi}(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
to be the groupoid consisting of pairs
((\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}),\mathds{T})
where
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is an object of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
, the pair
(\mathcal{E},\mathds{T})
is an object of
\mathbf{Twist}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}|_{T}}(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
, and
\mathds{T}
constitutes a set of tensors on
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
or, equivalently, each tensor in
\mathds{T}
is
Frobenius invariant
: fixed under the composition
\mathcal{E}\to\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{E}
, where the first map sends
x
to
x\otimes 1
.

Finally, observe that there is a natural identification

\phi^{\ast}\underline{\Isom}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{n},\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\underline{\Isom}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{n},\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}),

for an object
\mathcal{E}
of
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T,\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
. Moreover, there is an identification between
\underline{\Isom}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{n},\mathcal{E})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]
and
\underline{\Isom}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{n},\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}])
. So, if
((\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}),\mathds{T})
is an object of
\mathbf{Twist}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}|_{T}}^{\varphi}(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
, then the
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
-torsor
\underline{\Isom}((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(\mathcal{E},\mathds{T}))
has a natural Frobenius also denoted
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Our main Tannakian result concerning prismatic
F
-crystals is the following, where for a prism
(A,I)
the category
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(A,I)
has the obvious meaning.

Proposition 1.29
.

The natural functor

\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)\to\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T}\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(A,I),

is an equivalence. Moreover, we have a commuting triangle of equivalences

\textstyle{\mathbf{Twist}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}|_{T}}(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{((\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}),\mathds{T})\mapsto(\underline{\Isom}((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(\mathcal{E},\mathds{T})),\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}})\mapsto(\omega_{\mathcal{A}},\varphi_{\omega_{\mathcal{A}}})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\omega,\varphi_{\omega})\mapsto((\omega(\Lambda_{0}),\varphi_{\omega}),\omega(\mathds{T}_{0}))\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt}

Results from Appendix
A
(notably Proposition
A.18
applied to both
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
and
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]
) and Proposition
1.17
reduces the proof of this statement to the claim that the natural functor

\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)\to\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(A)

is an equivalence.

To prove this, we introduce an ancillary site. Denote by
\mathfrak{X}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
the subcategory of
\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
with the same objects as
\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
and with those morphisms
(A,I)\to(B,J)
with
\Spf(B)\to\Spf(A)
adically étale, with the induced topology. By Proposition
1.4
(and
[
SP
,
Tag 00X5
]
), the functor
\mathfrak{X}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
induces a morphism of sites, and there are equivalences of sites
\mathfrak{X}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}/(A,I)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Spf(A)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Proposition 1.30
.

The following restriction functors are equivalences of categories

\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}/T)\to\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(A).

Proof.

Fix an object
(A,I)
of
\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
and set
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/(A,I)
to be the full subcategory of
\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/(A,I)
consisting of covers
(A,I)\to(B,J)
, with the induced topology. We use this notation in a similar way for other sites. The naturally defined functor
\mu_{1}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/(A,I)\to\Spf(A)^{\prime}_{\mathrm{fl}}
is continuous and has exact pullback by
[
SP
,
Tag 00X6
]
and
[
DLMS24
, Lemma 3.3]
, and we have the equality
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/(A,I)}=\mu_{1\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{\Spf(A)})
. The same properties hold mutatis mutandis for
\mu_{3}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\Spf(A)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\prime}
. From the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/(A,I)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{1}}
\textstyle{\Spf(A)^{\prime}_{\mathrm{fl}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{2}}
\textstyle{\Spf(A)_{\mathrm{fl}}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}/(A,I)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{1}}
\textstyle{\Spf(A)^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{4}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{2}}
\textstyle{\Spf(A)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{3}}

(where the vertical functors are the natural inclusions), we obtain the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/(A,I))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{1}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\Spf(A)^{\prime}_{\mathrm{fl}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{2}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\Spf(A)_{\mathrm{fl}})}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}/(A,I))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{3}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{1}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\Spf(A)^{\prime}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{4}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{2}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\Spf(A)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{3}^{\ast}}

Note that
\mu_{4}^{\ast}
and
\mu_{2}^{\ast}
are evidently equivalences,
\nu_{2}^{\ast}
and
\nu_{3}^{\ast}
are equivalences by Corollary
A.11
, and
\mu_{3}^{\ast}
is an equivalence by above discussion. By a diagram chase we see that
\mu_{1}^{\ast}
is essentially surjective and so it is an equivalence by Corollary
A.6
. Thus,
\nu_{1}^{\ast}
is an equivalence. We deduce that for any object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
, its restriction to
(A,I)
can be trivialized on an étale cover, and thus the restriction
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}/T)
is an equivalence by Corollary
A.6
.

To prove that
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}/T)\to\twolim\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\Spf(A)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
is an equivalence we exhibit a quasi-inverse. For an object
(\mathcal{A}_{(A,I)})
of the 2-limit category define the object
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
to be the one sending
(A,I)
to
\mathcal{A}_{(A,I)}(A)
. This is a presheaf carrying an action of
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
and for which the action on
(A,I)
-points is simply transitive whenever non-empty. Thus, we are done as it is simple to see that this presheaf is locally isomorphic to
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.
∎

Finally, we mention the relationship between the above discussion and analytic prismatic
F
-crystals. By Theorem
A.19
, and the ideas applied in the proof of Proposition
1.29
, one has

(1.3.1)

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)=\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T}\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(U(A,I))=\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T}\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(U(A,I)),

and the restriction map

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(A)\to\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(U(A,I))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)

is fully faithful by
[
GR24
, Proposition 3.7]
,
where
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(U(A,I))
has the obvious meaning. For this reason, we occasionally identify
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}/T)
with the category
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
which, by definition, is the right-most term in (
1.3.1
).

1.3.2.
Quasi-syntomic torsors with
F
-structure

For the sake of completeness, we compare the material from §
1.3.1
to the analogous theory in the quasi-syntomic setting.

Abbreviate
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{pris}}}
to
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{pris}}
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{pris}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{pris}}}}]}
to
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{pris}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{pris}}}}]
. Write
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
instead of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{pris}}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
. We make similar notational conventions concerning
(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{PRIS}})
.

Proposition 1.31
.

The following are well-defined equivalences of categories functorial in
\mathcal{G}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emph{eval.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emph{res.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emph{eval.}}

Proof.

It suffices to show that
u_{\ast}
,
\mathrm{res.}
, and
\mathrm{eval.}\colon\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})\to\twolim\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
are equivalences. To prove that
\mathrm{eval.}
is an equivalence, take an open cover
\{\Spf(R_{i})\}
of
\mathfrak{X}
and a qrsp cover
R_{i}\to S_{i}
. Let
S_{i}^{\bullet}
denote the objects of the Čech nerve of
R_{i}\to S_{i}
, which consist of qrsp rings by
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.30]
. Then, by descent we have that the natural evaluation functor
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})\to\twolim\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{i}^{\bullet}})
is an equivalence, where we have implicitly used
[
ALB23
, Proposition 3.30]
. The fact that
\mathrm{eval.}
is an equivalence easily follows.

Set
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}
to have the same objects as
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}
, but whose morphisms are required to be quasi-syntomic. From
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.16]
and
[
SP
,
Tag 00X6
]
, the inclusions
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}
and
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}}
induce morphisms of sites. As
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}
has the same set of covers of
\mathfrak{X}
as
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}}
, from Corollary
A.6
we deduce that the functors
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}})\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
and
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
are equivalences, and so is their composition
\mathrm{res.}

By Corollary
A.7
to show that
u_{\ast}
is an equivalence, it suffices to show that for an object
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
, that
u_{\ast}(\mathcal{A})
is locally non-empty. Passing to a qrsp cover
\{\Spf(R_{i})\to\mathfrak{X}\}
we may assume that
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
where
R
is qrsp. By Proposition
1.30
,
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})=\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
. Pulling back
\mathcal{A}
along the closed immersion
i\colon\Spec(R)\to\Spec({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
as in
[
ALB23
, Theorem 3.29]
, gives a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
i^{\ast}\mathcal{A}
on
\Spf(R)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Let
R\to S
be a
p
-adically étale morphism such that
(i^{\ast}\mathcal{A})(S)
is non-empty. By Lemma
1.6
we see
S
is qrsp, and so
(u_{\ast}\mathcal{A})(S)=\mathcal{A}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{S})
. But, the pair
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},\ker({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}\to S))
is Henselian by
[
ALB23
, Lemma 4.28]
, and so
\mathcal{A}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S},I_{S})
is non-empty (e.g., by
[
BČ22
, Theorem 2.1.6]
).
∎

The proof of the following is obtained mutatis mutandis from the proof of Proposition
1.31
.

Proposition 1.32

(cf.
[
ALB23
, Proposition 4.4]
and
[
BS23
, Proposition 2.13 and Proposition 2.14]
)
.

The following are well-defined rank-preserving bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{PRIS}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{pris}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\mathbf{Vect}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emph{eval.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emph{res.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emph{eval.}}

Set
\phi=v_{\ast}(\phi)
on
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{pris}}=v_{\ast}(\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
. For an object
\mathcal{B}
of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
, define
\mathcal{B}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{pris}}}}]
as the pushforward along
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{pris}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{pris}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{pris}}}}]
, and
\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{B}\vcentcolon=\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{B}
. There are identifications
v_{\ast}\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{A}=\phi^{\ast}v_{\ast}\mathcal{A}
and
v_{\ast}(\mathcal{A}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}])=(v_{\ast}\mathcal{A})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{pris}}}}]
. Define a
quasi-syntomic
\mathcal{G}
-torsor with
F
-structure
to be a pair
(\mathcal{B},\varphi)
where
\mathcal{B}
is an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
and
\varphi
a
Frobenius isomorphism

\varphi\colon(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{B})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{pris}}}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{B}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{pris}}}}].

A morphism of quasi-syntomic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure is a morphism of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{pris}}
-torsors commuting with the Frobenii. Denote the category of such objects by
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
. One can similarly define the categories
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
and
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})
, as well as their analogues for
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}}
or
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}
.

Using Proposition
1.29
, Proposition
1.31
, and Proposition
1.32
one may deduce the following.

Proposition 1.33
.

There is a commuting diagram of well-defined equivalences

\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}})}

which is functorial in
\mathcal{G}
. Similar assertions hold with
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}
replaced by
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{QSYN}}
or
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}
.

2.
\mathcal{G}
-objects in the category of crystalline local systems

In this section we derive Tannakian analogues of the results of
[
BS23
]
,
[
GR24
]
, and
[
DLMS24
]
. Unless stated otherwise, we still use the notation given in Notation
1.1
.

2.1.
The category of
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local systems

In the statement of the main result of this section it will be helpful to have a clear notion of a
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system (on a scheme or an adic space).

2.1.1.
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local systems on a scheme

For a locally topologically Noetherian scheme
S
(in the sense in
[
BS15
, Definition 6.6.9]
), recall from
[
BS15
, Definition 4.1.1]
that a morphism of schemes
f\colon S^{\prime}\to S
is
weakly étale
if
f
and
\Delta_{f}
are flat. Moreover, we can associate to
S
the
proétale site

S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
which is the full subcategory of
S_{\mathrm{fpqc}}
consisting of weakly étale morphisms
S^{\prime}\to S
, with the induced topology.

We will be interested in the ringed site
(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{S})
, where

\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{S}\vcentcolon=R\varprojlim\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{S}=\varprojlim\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{S},

where the second equality follows from
[
BS15
, Proposition 3.2.3, Proposition 3.1.10, and Proposition 4.2.8]
. We often use the notation
\mathbb{Z}/p^{\infty}\vcentcolon=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. More generally, for a topological space
T
, denote by
\underline{T}_{S}
(or just
\underline{T}
when
S
is clear from context) the sheaf (see
[
BS15
, Lemma 4.2.12]
)

\underline{T}_{S}\colon S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{Set},\qquad U\mapsto\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(U,T).

If
T
is totally disconnected, then
\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(U,T)
is equal to
\text{Hom}_{\text{cont.}}(\pi_{0}(U),T)
, and so if
T=\varprojlim T_{i}
with each
T_{i}
finite then
\underline{T}=\varprojlim\underline{T_{i}}
where each
\underline{T_{i}}
is the constant sheaf.

Lemma 2.1
.

With notation as in
(
A.5.1
)
, there is an identification
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}
, compatible in
n
and functorial in
\mathcal{G}
, for
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

Proof.

We handle only the case where
n
is finite as the case for
n=\infty
follows by passing to the limit. We provide for
U
in
S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
an isomorphism
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}}(U)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}(U)
bi-functorial in
U
and
\mathcal{G}
and compatible in
n
. Let
\{D_{i}\}
be the set of discrete quotient spaces of
U
. Then, for any discrete space
X
there is an identification between
\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(U,X)
and
\varinjlim\Hom(D_{i},X)
, bi-functorial in
U
and
X
. As
\mathcal{G}
is locally of finite presentation over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}}(U)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}(\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(U,\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))

\displaystyle=\varinjlim\mathcal{G}(\Hom(D_{i},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))

\displaystyle=\varinjlim\text{Hom}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(A_{\mathcal{G}},\text{Hom}(D_{i},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})),

(cf.
[
SP
,
Tag 01ZC
]
), where
A_{\mathcal{G}}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{G})
. On the other hand we see that

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})}(U)

\displaystyle=\text{Hom}_{\text{cont.}}(U,\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))

\displaystyle=\varinjlim\text{Hom}(D_{i},\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))

\displaystyle=\varinjlim\Hom(D_{i},\Hom_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(A_{\mathcal{G}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})).

Thus, we are done via the natural isomorphism of groups

\Hom(D_{i},\Hom_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(A_{\mathcal{G}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))\to\text{Hom}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(A_{\mathcal{G}},\text{Hom}(D_{i},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}))

bi-functorial in
D_{i}
and
\mathcal{G}
, given by currying.
∎

Denote by
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(S)
the category
\mathbf{Vect}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}})
of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-local systems
. By
[
BS15
, Corollary 5.1.5 and Proposition 6.8.4]
, this is equivalent to
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(S_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
(see
[
SGA5
, Exposé VI]
) as an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category. We refer to objects of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(S)
as
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-local systems
.

It will also be convenient to consider the category
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-local systems
on
S
. This has the same objects as
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S)
, denoted by
\mathbb{L}
of
\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
when clarity is necessary, but with

(2.1.1)

\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)}(\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\mathbb{L}^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])\vcentcolon=\Gamma(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{H}om(\mathbb{L},\mathbb{L}^{\prime})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]),

where
\mathcal{H}om(\mathbb{L},\mathbb{L}^{\prime})
is the internal Hom in
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S)
. The category
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)
admits a fully faithful embedding into
\mathbf{Vect}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, and so inherits an exact
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structure.

Remark 2.2
.

The essential image of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)\to\mathbf{Vect}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, consists of those
\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-vector bundles which admit a
\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-lattice. Equivalently, assuming
S
is connected, this corresponds to the embedding
\mathbf{Rep}^{\mathrm{cont.}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s}))\to\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{cont.}}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s}))
, where
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})
is the proétale fundamental group as in
[
BS15
, §7]
and
\overline{s}
is a geometric point. If
S
is geometrically unibranch then
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})=\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})
(see
[
BS15
, Lemma 7.4.10]
), and so by compactness any representation
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})\to\GL_{n}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
factorizes through a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice and so
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)\to\mathbf{Vect}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
is an equivalence. But, this is not true in general (e.g. if
S
is the projective nodal curve, then
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}
with the discrete topology).

Proposition 2.3
.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the group
\mathcal{G}
is reconstructible in
(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}})
and every object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(S)
is locally trivial.

Proof.

Assume first that
S
is locally Noetherian. For the first statement, we deal only with the case
n=\infty
. Let
U
in
S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
be arbitrary. One may identify the natural map
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(U)\to\underline{\Aut}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}})(U)
with the map from
\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(\pi_{0}(U),\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
to the system
(g_{\Lambda})
of elements of
\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(\pi_{0}(U),\GL(\Lambda))
for
\Lambda
in
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
, which are compatible in
\Lambda
. The projection
(g_{\Lambda})\mapsto g_{\Lambda_{0}}
from
\underline{\Aut}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}})(U)
to
\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(\pi_{0}(U),\GL(\Lambda_{0}))
is injective as every object
\Lambda
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
is a subquotient of
\Lambda_{0}^{\otimes}
(see
[
dS09
, Proposition 12]
). As the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{\underline{\Aut}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}})(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(\pi_{0}(U),\GL(\Lambda_{0}))}

commutes, and
a
and
b
are injective, to show that
a
is an isomorphism it suffices to show that
\mathrm{im}(b)\subseteq\mathrm{im}(c)
. But, by functoriality
\mathrm{im}(b)
fixes
\mathds{T}
and thus lies in
\mathrm{im}(c)
.

Let
\omega
be an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S)
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
consider the sheaf
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})
, associating to a locally Noetherian
S
-scheme
f\colon T\to S
the set of isomorphisms
\omega_{\mathrm{triv}}\to\omega_{n,T}
, where
\omega_{n,T}(\Lambda)\vcentcolon=f^{-1}(\omega(\Lambda)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}})
is considered as an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(T)
. To prove the second claim it suffices to show that
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{\infty})
is representable by a weakly étale cover of
S
. Moreover, as
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{\infty})
is the limit of
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})
(cf.
[
BS15
, Proposition 6.8.4]
) it further suffices to show that
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})
is represented by a finite
étale cover of
S
for every
n
in
\mathbb{N}
. To see this, observe that the natural map

\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})\to\underline{\Isom}(\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}},\omega_{n}(\Lambda_{0}))

is a closed embedding, cut out by the intersection of the following conditions (with terminology from
[
dS09
, Definition 10 and Proposition 12]
) on an
f
in
\underline{\Isom}(\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}},\omega_{n}(\Lambda_{0}))
: for every tuple
(a,b,W,U,q)
, where
a,b
are multi-indices,
W
is a special subrepresentation of
(\Lambda_{0})^{a}_{b}
, and
q\colon W\to U
is a surjection of representations,
f
and
f^{-1}
preserve
W
and
\ker q
. Thus,
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})\to S
is finite. Moreover, by the topological invariance of the pro-étale topos (see
[
BS15
, Lemma 5.4.2]
), pullback along
i\colon\Spec(A/I)\to\Spec(A)
, for a square-zero ideal
I
of a Noetherian ring
A
, defines an equivalence

i^{-1}\colon\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\Spec(A))\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\Spec(A/I))

for
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. From this we deduce that
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})\to S
is formally étale in the sense of
[
SP
,
Tag 02HG
]
, and thus finite étale by
[
SP
,
Tag 02HM
]
.

To show that
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})\to S
is surjective, it suffices to show that the pullback to each geometric point of
S
is a trivial torsor, and so we may assume that
S
is the spectrum of an algebraically closed field. In this case, there is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence between
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(S)
and
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
. But, by Theorem
A.19
, if
\nu_{n}
belongs to
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
then
\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\nu_{n})
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor. But,
H^{1}(\Spec(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}),\mathcal{G})
is trivial (e.g. by
[
Mil17
, Corollary 17.98]
and
[
BČ22
, Theorem 2.1.6]
), and so the claim follows.

To remove the locally Noetherian hypothesis, we may proceed as follows. We may assume that
n
is in
\mathbb{N}
and
S
is connected. Consider
W
in
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
with a saturated injection
W\hookrightarrow A_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{G}),\mathbb{Z}_{p})
whose image in
A_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}/p^{n}
contains
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
. We may replace
S
by a finite étale connected cover that trivializes
\omega_{n}(W)
. We claim then that
\omega_{n}(V)
is trivial for all
V
. By the locally Noetherian case, for each
s
in
S
, we have an isomorphism

\psi\colon\underline{\Isom}(\omega_{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{n})_{s}\times^{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}W(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})\simeq\omega_{n}(W)_{s}.

Take a trivial
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-torsor
\mathcal{T}
over
S
and an isomorphism
\mathcal{T}\times^{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}W(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})\simeq\omega_{n}(W)
extending
\psi
. For an object
V
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
and
v
in
V(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, let
f_{v}\colon A_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}\to V
be the morphism induced by the map
\mathcal{G}\to V
sending
g
to
gv
. Let
\mathrm{ev}_{1}
in
A_{\mathcal{G}}^{*}/p^{n}
be the unit section. Then

\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{\id\times\mathrm{ev}_{1}}\mathcal{T}\times^{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}W(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})\simeq\omega_{n}(W)\xrightarrow{\omega_{n}(f_{v}|_{W})}\omega_{n}(V)

gives a map
\mathcal{T}\times^{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}V(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})\to\omega_{n}(V)
. One checks this is an isomorphism over
s
, and so is an isomorphism as the source and target are finite étale over
S
.
∎

Given Lemma
2.1
, we write
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
instead of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}}}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
, and call objects of this category
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-torsors
. By Proposition
2.3
and Proposition
A.18
we know that there is a natural equivalence of categories between
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(S)
and
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
. Explicitly, this functor associates to a
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-torsor
\mathcal{A}
the
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-local system
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
given by
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\Lambda)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{A}\wedge^{\mathcal{G}}\underline{\Lambda}
.

By a
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-local system
on
S
, we mean an exact
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G)\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)
, the category of which we denote by
G\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)
. The following trivial observation allows us to define a natural functor
(-)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\colon\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S)\to G\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(S)
. Let us denote by
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
the category which has the same objects as
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
, denoted by either
\Lambda
or
\Lambda[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
for clarity, but where we set
\Hom_{\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}(\Lambda[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\Lambda^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
to be
\mathbf{Hom}_{\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})}(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
.

Lemma 2.4
.

The functor

\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G),\qquad\Lambda\mapsto\Lambda[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],

is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{\prime}
be objects of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
, viewed as right comodules of
A_{\mathcal{G}}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}_{G}(\mathcal{G})
and view
\Lambda[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
and
\Lambda^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
as right comodules of
A_{\mathcal{G}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]=\mathcal{O}_{G}(G)
. Then, it suffices to show that under the natural isomorphism
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to\Hom_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\Lambda[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\Lambda^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
that the subsets of comodule homomorphisms are matched. But, this is obvious as
A_{\mathcal{G}}
is a flat
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module. To show that this functor is essentially surjective, let
V
be an
A_{\mathcal{G}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
-comodule. An
A_{\mathcal{G}}
-comodule lattice is obtained using
[
Bro13
, Lemma 3.1]
applied to
V
, by taking an
A_{\mathcal{G}}
-comodule, locally free (and thus free) as a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module, containing any given
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice of
V
.
∎

Finally, it is useful to have a more concrete description of torsors for a pro-finite group
H
on
S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. We set the following notation.

Notation 2.5
.

Let
H=\varprojlim H_{n}
be a pro-finite group, where each
H_{n}
is a finite group.

Consider a projective system of schemes
\{X_{n}\}
, each equipped with the structure of a principal homogeneous space over
S
for
H_{n}
(see
[
SP
,
Tag 049A
]
). The quotient sheaf
X_{n}/K_{n}
, where
K_{n}\vcentcolon=\ker(H_{n}\to H_{n-1})
, is representable by
[
SGA3-1
, Exposé V, §7, Théorème 7.1]
, and
X_{n}/K_{n}\to S
is naturally a principal homogeneous space for
H_{n-1}
. We say
(X_{n})
is an
H
-covering
if each
X_{n}\to X_{n-1}
is
K_{n}
-equivariant (when the target is given the trivial action) and the induced map
X_{n}/K_{n}\to X_{n-1}
is an
H_{n-1}
-equivariant isomorphism. A morphism of
H
-coverings
(X_{n})\to(Y_{n})
is a compatible family of
H_{n}
-equivariant morphisms
X_{n}\to Y_{n}
.

An
S
-scheme
X
equipped with an action of
H
is a
principal homogeneous space
for
H
if
h_{X}
with the induced action of
\underline{H}
is an
\underline{H}
-torsor. Morphisms of principal homogeneous spaces are
H
-equivariant morphisms of
S
-schemes. If
(X_{n})
is an
H
-covering then
X_{\infty}\vcentcolon=\varprojlim X_{n}
is a principal homogeneous space for
H
. Conversely, if
X
is a principal homogeneous space for
H
, the system
(X_{n})
with
X_{n}\vcentcolon=X/K_{n}
(a scheme by Proposition
A.10
) is an
H
-covering with
X_{\infty}=X
.

Applying
[
BS15
, Lemma 7.3.9]
to
\mathcal{T}\times^{\underline{H}}\underline{H}_{n}
for every
n
, every object
\mathcal{T}
of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\underline{H}}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
is representable. We summarize the above as follows.

Proposition 2.6
.

For locally topologically Noetherian
S
, the following are equivalences:

\left\{\begin{matrix}H\emph{-coverings}\\
\emph{of}S\end{matrix}\right\}\xrightarrow{(X_{n})\mapsto X_{\infty}}\left\{\begin{matrix}\emph{Principalhomogenous}\\
\emph{spacesfor}H\emph{on}S\end{matrix}\right\}\xrightarrow{X\mapsto h_{X}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\underline{H}}(S_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}).

2.1.2.
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local systems on an adic space

Let
X
be a locally Noetherian adic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(cf.
[
Sch13
, p. 17]
), and let
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
be the pro-étale site as in
[
BMS18
, §5.1]
. As in
[
Sch13
]
, we call an object of
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}

affinoid perfectoid
if it can be represented as
(\Spa(R_{i},R_{i}^{+}))
with finite étale surjective transition maps, and the Huber pair
(R,R^{+})=((\varinjlim R_{i})^{\wedge},(\varinjlim R_{i}^{+})^{\wedge})
(endowed with the unique topology such that each
R_{i}\to R
is adic) is perfectoid. In this case,
\Spa(R,R^{+})\sim\varprojlim\Spa(R_{i},R_{i}^{+})
(see
[
SW13
, §2.4]
). The affinoid perfectoid objects of
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
form a basis of
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(cf.
[
Sch13
, Proposition 4.8]
). The site
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
is not subcanonical (see
[
ALY21
, Example 4.1.7]
), but
h_{Y}
and
h_{Y}^{\#}
have the same value on affinoid perfectoid objects (see
[
ALY21
, Proposition 4.1.8]
). So, we abusively conflate the two.

We will again be interested in the ringed site
(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{X})
, where

\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{X}=R\varprojlim\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{X}=\varprojlim\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{X}\in\mathbf{Shv}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}),

where
\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{X}
is the constant sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
associated to
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
, and the second equality follows now from
[
Sch13
, Proposition 8.2]
. Again, more generally, for a topological space
T
, we denote by
\underline{T}_{X}
(or just
\underline{T}
when
X
is clear from context) the sheaf
7
7

7

That this is a sheaf can be deduced from the fact that if
\{Y_{i}\to Y\}
is a cover, then
\coprod_{i}|Y_{i}|\to|Y|
is a quotient map. By
[
Sch22
, Lemma 2.5]
), to prove this it suffices to each
|Y_{i}|\to|Y|
is generalizing, but this follows by combining
[
Hub96
, Lemma 1.1.10]
and
[
Sch22
, Lemma 2.11]
.

\underline{T}_{X}\colon X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{Set},\qquad Y\mapsto\text{Hom}_{\text{cont.}}(Y,T).

If
T
is totally disconnected, then
\Hom_{\text{cont.}}(Y,T)
equals
\text{Hom}_{\text{cont.}}(\pi_{0}(Y),T)
, and if
T=\varprojlim T_{i}
with each
T_{i}
finite then
\underline{T}=\varprojlim\underline{T_{i}}
where each
\underline{T_{i}}
is the constant sheaf.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, write
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(X)
for the category
\mathbf{Vect}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}})
of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-local systems
. By
[
Sch13
, Proposition 8.2]
, this category is equivalent to
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
as an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category. The objects of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(X)
are called
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-local systems
. The following is proven, mutatis mutandis, as in Lemma
2.1
and (the locally Noetherian case of) Proposition
2.3
(or alternatively, one can use Remark
2.10
and Proposition
2.11
, which is proven independently).

Proposition 2.7
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, there is an identification
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}
, the group
\mathcal{G}
is reconstructible in
(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}})
, and every object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(X)
is locally trivial.

It will again be convenient to consider the category
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-local systems
on
X
, defined again using a formula as in (
2.1.1
). The category
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
admits a fully faithful embedding into
\mathbf{Vect}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, and so inherits an exact
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structure from this embedding

Remark 2.8
.

As in Remark
2.2
, the embedding
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)\to\mathbf{Vect}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
is not generally essentially surjective. The same reasoning applies, except now the relevant non-compact group is the de Jong fundamental group
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{dJ}}(X,\overline{x})
(see
[
dJ95b
]
and
[
ALY21
, Corollary 4.4.2]
). Here the situation is more extreme though, as even for
X=\mathbb{P}^{1,\mathrm{an}}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
the de Jong fundamental group is non-compact (cf.
[
dJ95b
, Proposition 7.4]
).

Given Proposition
2.7
, we write
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
instead of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
, and call objects of this category
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-torsors
. Moreover, by Proposition
A.18
we know that there is a natural equivalence of categories between
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(X)
and
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
. Explicitly, this functor associates to a
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-torsor
\mathcal{A}
the
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-local sytem
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}
given by
\omega_{\mathcal{A}}(\Lambda)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{P}\wedge^{\mathcal{G}}\Lambda
.

We again define the category of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-local systems on
X
, denoted
G\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
, to be the category of exact
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functors
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G)\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
. Again by Lemma
2.4
, we obtain a natural functor
(-)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\colon\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\to G\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
.

Finally, as in §
2.1.1
, it is convenient to have a more down-to-earth definition of torsor for a pro-finite group. We use Notation
2.5
. The definition of an
H
-covering
(Y_{n})
and principal homogeneous spaces
Y
for
H
is verbatim to the case of schemes, and the proof of the next result is obtained mutatis mutandis from that of Proposition
2.6
(cf.
[
ALY21
, Theorem 4.4.1]
).

Proposition 2.9
.

The following functors are equivalences functorial in
H
:

\left\{\begin{matrix}H\emph{-coverings}\\
\emph{of}X\end{matrix}\right\}\xrightarrow{(Y_{n})\mapsto Y_{\infty}}\left\{\begin{matrix}\emph{Principalhomogenous}\\
\emph{spacesfor}H\emph{on}X\end{matrix}\right\}\xrightarrow{Y\mapsto h_{Y}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\underline{H}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}).

2.1.3.
Comparison via analytification

Fix a non-archimedean extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
S
to be a finite type
K
-scheme, and
H
as in Notation
2.5
. Consider the analytification
S^{\mathrm{an}}\vcentcolon=S\times_{\Spec(K)}\Spa(K)
of
S
(see
[
Hub94
, Proposition 3.8]
). By
[
Lüt93
, Theorem 3.1]
, we have an equivalence

\left\{\begin{matrix}H\text{-coverings}\\
\text{of }S\end{matrix}\right\}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\left\{\begin{matrix}H\text{-coverings}\\
\text{of }S^{\mathrm{an}}\end{matrix}\right\},\qquad(Y_{n})\mapsto(Y_{n}^{\mathrm{an}}).

On the other hand, using the morphism of sites
S^{\mathrm{an}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to S_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
as in
[
Hub96
, §3.8]
, we have a functor

(-)^{\mathrm{an}}\colon\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S)\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S^{\mathrm{an}}),

which is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence by
[
Lüt93
, Theorem 3.1]
and the density of classical points. This induces an equivalence of
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local systems agreeing with the above equivalence of
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-coverings under the equivalences of Proposition
2.6
and Proposition
2.9
. We denote the quasi-inverse of
(-)^{\mathrm{an}}
by
(-)^{\mathrm{alg}}
.

2.1.4.
Comparison on affinoids

Let
H
be as in Notation
2.5
. For a strongly Noetherian Huber pair
(A,A^{+})
, we have, by
[
Hub96
, Example 1.6.6 ii)]
, an equivalence

\left\{\begin{matrix}H\text{-coverings}\\
\text{of }\Spec(A)\end{matrix}\right\}\to\left\{\begin{matrix}H\text{-coverings}\\
\text{of }\Spa(A,A^{+})\end{matrix}\right\}.

If
(A,A^{+})
is topologically of finite type over a non-archimedean extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, this gives

\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spec(A))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spa(A,A^{+})),

which is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence by the density of classical points. If
\Spec(A)
is connected, then the choice of a geometric point
\overline{x}
gives a futher bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spec(A))\to\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{cont.}}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\Spec(A),\overline{x})),\qquad\mathbb{L}\mapsto\mathbb{L}_{\overline{x}},

(see
[
SGA5
, Exposé VI, Proposition 1.2.5]
). We often tacitly identify a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
\Spa(A,A^{+})
with such a representation.

2.1.5.
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local systems on a diamond

It will be convenient to interpret the notion of a
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
-local system in terms of the language of diamonds as in
[
Sch22
, §11]
.
Let
Y
be a diamond in the sense in
[
Sch22
, Definition 11.1]
and
Y_{\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}}
denote the quasi-pro-étale site as in
[
Sch22
, Definition 14.1 (ii)]
.
Similarly as in §
2.1.1
–
2.1.2
, for a topological space
T
, we consider the associated sheaf
\underline{T}_{Y}
(as in
[
Sch22
, Example 11.12]
). In particular, we have the ringed site
(Y_{\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{Y})
and the sheaf of groups
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}_{Y}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
Write
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(Y)
for the category
\mathbf{Vect}(Y_{\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}})
.

Remark 2.10
.

Let
X
be a locally Noetherian adic space
X
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
X^{\lozenge}
denote the diamond associated to
X
(see
[
Sch22
, Definition 15.5 and Lemma 15.6]
).
Then the morphism of sites
X^{\lozenge}_{\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}}\to X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear bi-exact
\otimes
-equivalence

\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(X)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(X^{\lozenge})

and an equivalence of groupoids

\mathbf{Tors}_{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}}(X)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}}(X^{\lozenge}).

Indeed, when
n\in\mathbb{N}
, any of the above four categories is identified with the corresponding category for the étale site:
note first that the sheaf
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}
on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
X^{\lozenge}_{\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}}
) is the pullback of the constant sheaf
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
on
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
X^{\lozenge}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
);
then for the left (resp. right) hand side, use the version of
[
Sch13
, Corollary 3.17 (i)]
for sheaves of sets, which is shown by the same way as
[
Sch22
, Proposition 8.5 (i)]
, (resp. use
[
Sch22
, Proposition 14.8]
) to see that the category of étale local systems/étale torsors is fully-faithfully embedded;
then use the version of
[
Sch13
, Lemma 3.16]
for sheaves of sets, again shown as in
[
Sch22
, Proposition 8.5 (ii)]
, (resp. use
[
Sch22
, Proposition 9.7, cf. Definition 10.1]
) to see that any proétale (resp. quasi-proétale)
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-local system or
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}
-torsor is étale locally trivialized.
Then the equivalences follow from
[
Sch22
, Lemma 15.6]
. By passage to limit we obtain the case of
n=\infty
, using the analogues of
[
BS15
, Proposition 6.8.4 (i)]
, which holds as any
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}
-torsor on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
X^{\lozenge}_{\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}}
) is trivialized by a finite étale cover.

Proposition 2.11
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, there is an identification
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})}
, the group
\mathcal{G}
is reconstructible in
(Y_{\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}},\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}})
, and every object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(Y)
is locally trivial.

Proof.

The identification and the reconstructibility are proven mutatis mutandis as in Lemma
2.1
and (the locally Noetherian case of) Proposition
2.3
.

To prove the local triviality, we may assume that
Y
is represented by a perfectoid space, in which case the assertion is proven in the proof of
[
SW20
, Proposition 22.6.1]
.
∎

2.1.6.
Comparison with
v
-sheaves

There is a unique functor

(-)^{\mathrm{ad}}\colon\left\{\begin{matrix}p\text{-adic formal}\\
\text{schemes}\end{matrix}\right\}\to\left\{\begin{matrix}\text{Pre-adic spaces}\\
\text{over }\mathbb{Z}_{p}\end{matrix}\right\},

sending open covers to open covers, and with
\Spf(A)^{\mathrm{ad}}=\Spa^{Y}(A,A)
(see
[
SW20
, §3.4]
). For a
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{Y}
, denote by
\mathfrak{Y}^{\lozenge}
the
v
-sheaf over
\Spd(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
associated to
\mathfrak{Y}^{\mathrm{ad}}
as in
[
SW20
, §18.1]
. Set
(\mathfrak{Y}^{\lozenge})_{\eta}\vcentcolon=\mathfrak{Y}^{\lozenge}\times_{\Spd(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\Spd(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. For an affine open
\Spf(A)\subseteq\mathfrak{Y}
, the pair
(A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\widetilde{A})
is a Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, with
\widetilde{A}
the integral closure of
A
in
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
and
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
given the unique ring topology with the image of
A
in
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
open. The diamond
(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta})^{\lozenge}
associated to
\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta}\vcentcolon=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits\Spa^{Y}(A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\widetilde{A})
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(see
[
SW20
, §10.1]
) agrees with
(\mathfrak{Y}^{\lozenge})_{\eta}
. Denote the common object by
\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta}^{\lozenge}
.

Consider the quasi-pro-étale site
\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta,\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}}^{\lozenge}
as in
[
Sch22
, Definition 14.1]
. Consider

\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{\mathfrak{Y}}=R\lim\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{\mathfrak{Y}}=\lim\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{\mathfrak{Y}}

where
\underline{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
is the constant sheaf, and the second equality follows from
[
Sch22
, Lemma 7.18]
and
[
BS15
, Proposition 3.1.10 and Proposition 3.2.3]
. Define
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta})
to be
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta,\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}}^{\lozenge},\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{\mathfrak{Y}})
.

If
\mathfrak{Y}\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is locally of finite type, then
\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta}
is the rigid
K
-space associated to
\mathfrak{Y}
(see
[
Hub96
, §1.9]
or
[
FK18
, §A.5]
). Thus, in this case we have already defined an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta})
, and so there is potential for ambiguity. But, with
H
as in Notation
2.5
, defining
H
-coverings in the obvious way, one may again show that there is an equivalence of categories between such
H
-coverings and
\mathbf{Loc}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta})
.
Note
[
Sch22
, Lemma 15.6]
gives

\left\{\begin{matrix}H\text{-coverings}\\
\text{of }\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta}\end{matrix}\right\}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\left\{\begin{matrix}H\text{-coverings}\\
\text{of }\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta}^{\lozenge}\end{matrix}\right\},\qquad(Y_{n})\mapsto(Y_{n}^{\lozenge}).

Using this equivalence and
[
Sch22
, Proposition 14.3]
(for the
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-local system for each
n\geqslant 1
) one obtains a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta,\mathrm{qpro\acute{e}t}}^{\lozenge},\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{\mathfrak{Y}}).

Thus, no abmiguity actually occurs if
\mathfrak{Y}\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is locally of finite type.

If
\mathfrak{Y}
is quasi-syntomic, then there is a natural functor

\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\eta})\to\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{Y}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spf(R)_{\eta}).

This is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence by the next lemma and
[
Sch22
, Proposition 9.7]
, which we apply by using a limit argument to reduce to the case of finite coefficients.

Lemma 2.12
.

Let
\{\mathfrak{Y}_{i}\to\mathfrak{Y}\}
be a faithfully flat cover of
p
-adic formal schemes. Then, the collection
\{\mathfrak{Y}_{i,\eta}^{\lozenge}\to\mathfrak{Y}^{\lozenge}_{\eta}\}
is a cover of
v
-sheaves.

Proof.

By
[
Sch22
, Lemma 12.11]
it suffices to show that
\bigsqcup_{i}|\mathfrak{Y}_{i,\eta}^{\lozenge}|\to|\mathfrak{X}_{\eta}^{\lozenge}|
is surjective and any quasi-compact open of the target is covered by a quasi-compact open of the source. By
[
Sch22
, Lemma 15.6]
this is equivalent to proving this claim for
\bigsqcup_{i}|\mathfrak{Y}_{i,\eta}|\to|\mathfrak{X}_{\eta}|
. This reduces to showing that if
\Spf(B)\to\Spf(A)
is faithfully flat then
\Spf(B)_{\eta}\to\Spf(A)_{\eta}
is surjective. By
[
SW13
, Proposition 2.1.6]
, we must show that for an affinoid field
(K,K^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and a morphism
\Spf(K^{+})\to\Spf(A)
there exists a surjection
\Spf(L^{+})\to\Spf(K^{+})
, with
(L,L^{+})
an affinoid field over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, so that
\Spf(L^{+})\to\Spf(A)
lifts to
\Spf(B)
. This follows from the discussion in
[
ČS24
, Example (2), §2.2.1]
, as the argument there does not use that
K^{+}
has rank at most
1
.
∎

2.2.
The étale realization functor

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic flat formal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme, and
X=\mathfrak{X}_{\eta}^{\lozenge}
. We discuss the equivalence between
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems on
X
and prismatic Laurent
F
-crystals on
\mathfrak{X}
given in
[
BS23
, Corollary 3.7]
, explicating its bi-exactness.

Note that
\phi\colon\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
induces a morphism
\phi\colon\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p}\to\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p}
.
8
8

8

For
(A,I)
, the map
\phi_{A}
induces an endomorphism of
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]/p^{n}A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]
as
\phi_{A}(d)=d^{p}+p\delta(d)
for any
d
in
I
, which as
p
is nilpotent in
A/p^{n}
, implies that the image of
I
under
\phi_{A}\colon A/p^{n}\to A/p^{n}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]
generates the unit ideal.
As in
[
BS23
, Definition 3.2]
, define the category
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
of
prismatic Laurent
F
-crystals on
\mathfrak{X}
to consist of pairs
(\mathcal{L},\varphi)
with
\mathcal{L}
an object of
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
and
\varphi\colon\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{L}
an isomorphism in
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
, called the
Frobenius
, and morphisms are morphisms in
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
commuting with the Frobenii.

We can endow
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
with the structure of an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category, where it inherits its exact structure from
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
. By
[
BS23
, Proposition 2.7]
, together with an argument as in the proof of Proposition
1.17
, with
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
having the obvious meaning, taking global sections gives a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]^{\wedge}_{p}),

where the right-hand side is given the term-by-term exact and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structure.

In
[
BS23
, §3]
, Bhatt and Scholze define an
étale realization functor

T_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X).

which is an equivalence by
[
BS23
, Corollary 3.8]
(cf.
[
MW25
]
and
[
Wu21
]
).
When
\mathfrak{X}
is clear from context, we shall drop
\mathfrak{X}
from the notation. We now wish to show the following.

Proposition 2.13
.

The functor
T_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence.

Our proof essentially recounts the construction of Bhatt–Scholze, verifying exactness at each step. Let
R
be a qrsp ring, and define

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R,\infty}\vcentcolon=(\varinjlim_{\phi}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})^{\wedge}_{(p,I_{R})},\quad I_{R,\infty}=I_{R}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R,\infty}.

Then,
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R,\infty},I_{R,\infty})
is a perfect prism and the
p
-adically complete
R
-algebra

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R,\infty}/I_{R,\infty}=(\varinjlim_{\phi}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}/I_{R}\varinjlim_{\phi}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})^{\wedge}_{p}=\vcentcolon R_{\mathrm{perfd}}

is perfectoid and initial amongst maps from
R
to a perfectoid ring (see
[
BS22
, Corollary 7.3]
). Thus, the maps
\Spf(R_{\mathrm{perfd}})^{\lozenge}\to\Spf(R)^{\lozenge}
and
\Spf(R_{\mathrm{perfd}})^{\lozenge}_{\eta}\to\Spf(R)^{\lozenge}_{\eta}
are isomorphisms: indeed, for a perfectoid
(A,A^{+})
, by using
[
SW13
, Proposition 2.1.6 (i)]
the set
\Spf(R)^{\lozenge}((A,A^{+}))
is identified with the set
\Hom((R,R),(A,A^{+}))
of maps of Huber pairs, which is nothing but the set
\Hom(R,A^{+})
of maps of
p
-adic rings; similarly for
\Spf(R_{\mathrm{perfd}})^{\lozenge}
.

We now briefly recall the construction of the étale realization functor
T_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. When
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(S)
with
S
a perfectoid
p
-adic ring, the equivalence

\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})=\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I_{S}}}]_{p}^{\wedge})

is defined by passing to the tilt
X^{\flat}=\Spf(S^{\flat})_{\eta}
and applying the scalar extension along
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to W(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\flat},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
(see
[
BS23
, Example 3.5]
for the details).
When
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
with
R
a qrsp
p
-adic ring, the equivalence
T_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is defined via the isomorphism
\Spf(R_{\mathrm{perfd}})_{\eta}^{\lozenge}\to\Spf(R)^{\lozenge}_{\eta}
and the scalar extension functor

\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I_{R}}}]_{p}^{\wedge})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R,\infty}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I_{R,\infty}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}),

which is checked to be an equivalence by passing to mod
p
and using
[
BS23
, Proposition 3.6]
.
In general, for a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
, the equivalence
T_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is defined via quasi-syntomic descent (cf. Lemma
2.12
).

Lemma 2.14

(cf.
[
BS23
, Proposition 3.6 and Corollary 3.7]
)
.

Let
R
be a
p
-torsion-free qrsp ring. Then, the base change functor

\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I_{R}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R,\infty}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I_{R,\infty}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})

is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence functorial in
R
.

Proof.

We follow the notation of
[
BS23
, Proposition 3.6 and Corollary 3.7]
. The only thing to be verified is that this equivalence is bi-exact. In turn, by the method of proof in loc. cit. we reduce to the claim that the equivalence
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(S)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(S)
for any
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme
S
from
[
BS23
, Proposition 3.4]
is bi-exact. But, this is clear as the map
\mathbb{F}_{p}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
is faithfully flat.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.13
.

The only thing to verify is bi-exactness. As the proof in
[
BS23
, Corollary 3.7]
proceeds by descent to the case when
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
with
R
qrsp and
p
-torsion-free, which preserves exactness (cf. Lemma
2.12
), we also reduce to this case. Further, using Lemma
2.14
and the isomorphism
\Spf(R_{\mathrm{perfd}})^{\lozenge}_{\eta}\to\Spf(R)^{\lozenge}_{\eta}
we may reduce to the case when
R
is perfectoid.

By
[
BMS18
, Lemma 3.21]
,
(R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],R^{\prime})
is a Tate perfectoid algebra, where
R^{\prime}
is the integral closure of
R
in
R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
. Thus, there is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spa(R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],R^{\prime}))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spa(R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],R^{\prime})^{\flat})=\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spa(R^{\flat}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varpi^{\flat}}}],(R^{\prime})^{\flat}))

(see
[
Sch22
, Theorem 6.3]
), where
\varpi
is a pseudo-uniformizer of
R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
as in
[
SW20
, Lemma 6.2.2]
which may be taken to lie in
R
. On the other hand,
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}=W(R^{\flat})
and
I_{R}=(\tilde{\xi})
, with
\tilde{\xi}=p+[\varpi^{\flat}]\alpha
with
\alpha
in
W(R^{\flat})
(see
[
SW20
, Lemma 6.2.10]
). But,
W(R^{\flat})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}]^{\wedge}_{p}=W(R^{\flat}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varpi^{\flat}}}])
as both are strict
p
-rings (in the sense of
[
KL15
, Definition 3.2.1]
) with the same residue ring. Thus, we will be done if the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence between the category of
\varphi
-modules for
W(R^{\flat}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varpi^{\flat}}}])
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems on
\Spec(R^{\flat}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varpi^{\flat}}}])
is bi-exact, which was already discussed in the proof of Lemma
2.14
.
∎

Example 2.15
.

Let
R
be a base
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra. As
\widetilde{\theta}\colon\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\check{R})\to\check{R}
is
\Gamma_{R}
-equivariant, we see that
\Gamma_{R}
acts on
(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\check{R}),(\tilde{\xi}))
as an object of
R_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
. In this way, from a prismatic Laurent
F
-crystal
\mathcal{L}
on
R
, we obtain a finite free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module
\Lambda=\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\check{R}),(\tilde{\xi}))^{\varphi=1}
with a continuous action of
\Gamma_{R}
, which is the
\Gamma_{R}
-representation associated to
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{L})
.

2.3.
Crystalline local systems and analytic prismatic
F
-crystals

We discuss the equivalence from
[
GR24
]
and
[
DLMS24
]
and its Tannakian consequences. Unless stated otherwise, we assume that
\mathfrak{X}\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is smooth and write
X=\mathfrak{X}_{\eta}
.

2.3.1.
Filtered
F
-isocrystals

In this subsection we record our notation and conventions concerning the crystalline site,
F
-(iso)crystals, and filtered
F
-isocrystals. The reader is encouraged to skip this on first reading, referring back only as is necessary.

PD thickenings of formal schemes

Let
\mathfrak{Z}\to\Spf(W)
be an adic morphism. By a
PD thickening
of formal
\mathfrak{Z}
-schemes over
W
, we mean a pair
(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
where
\mathfrak{U}\to\mathfrak{Z}
is an adic morphism of formal
W
-schemes,
i\colon\mathfrak{U}\to\mathfrak{T}
is a closed immersion of adic formal
W
-schemes, and if
\mathcal{I}\vcentcolon=\ker(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}\to i_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{U}})
then
\gamma=(\gamma_{n})\colon\mathcal{I}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}
is a sequence of morphisms of sheaves so that for every open
\mathfrak{V}\subseteq\mathfrak{T}
the maps
\gamma_{n}\colon\mathcal{I}(\mathfrak{V})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}(\mathfrak{V})
form a PD structure compatible with the usual one on
(p)\subseteq W
. We will often drop
i
and
\gamma
from the notation when they are clear from context.

A morphism
(f,g)\colon(i^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T}^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})\to(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
is a morphism
f\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{U}
of formal
\mathfrak{Z}
-schemes, and
g\colon\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{T}
a morphism of formal
W
-schemes, such that the diagram

(2.3.1)

\textstyle{\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{T},}

commutes, and the induced map
g\colon\mathcal{I}\to g_{\ast}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
satisfies
g\circ\gamma=g_{\ast}(\gamma^{\prime})\circ g
. A morphism
(f,g)
is
Cartesian
if (
2.3.1
) is Cartesian, and a collection
\{(f_{i},g_{i})\colon(i_{i}\colon\mathfrak{U}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T}_{i},\gamma_{i})\to(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)\}
is a
flat cover
if each
(f_{i},g_{i})
is Cartesian and
\{g_{i}\colon\mathfrak{T}_{i}\to\mathfrak{T}\}
is a cover in
\Spf(W)_{\mathrm{fl}}
.

If
\mathfrak{U}=\Spf(B)
and
\mathfrak{T}=\Spf(A)
, we will often write
(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
as
(i\colon A\twoheadrightarrow B,\gamma)
, and write morphisms of such affine objects in the direction dictated by maps of rings. If
i\colon A\to A
is the identity map, we further shorten
(i\colon A\to A,\gamma)
to
A
. Moreover, for a PD thickening of affine formal
\mathfrak{Z}
-schemes
(i\colon A\twoheadrightarrow B,\gamma)
, we have a filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{PD}}(A)
, or just
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{PD}}
when
A
is clear from context, given by
\Fil^{r}_{\mathrm{PD}}(A)\vcentcolon=\ker(i)^{[r]}
. Here for an element
a
of
A
we sometimes abbreviate
\gamma_{r}(a)
to
a^{[r]}
, and for an ideal
I\subseteq A
by
I^{[r]}
the ideal generated by
\gamma_{e_{1}}(i_{1})\cdots\gamma_{e_{k}}(i_{k})
with
\sum e_{j}\geqslant r
and
i_{j}
in
I
for all
j
.

The big crystalline site of a formal scheme

The
(big) crystalline site

(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
is the site consisting of PD-thickenings of formal
\mathfrak{Z}
-schemes over
W
, endowed with the topology whose covers are flat covers. If
p
is locally nilpotent on
\mathfrak{Z}
, this coincides with the crystalline site as in
[
SP
,
Tag 07I5
]
, and in general the proof that
(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
is a site is proven in much the same way. The site
(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
naturally comes equipped with the sheaves
\mathcal{J}_{(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}
where

\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}(\mathfrak{T}),\qquad\mathcal{J}_{(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)\vcentcolon=\ker\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}\to i_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{U}}\right),

so
\mathcal{J}_{(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}(i\colon A\twoheadrightarrow B,\gamma)=\Fil^{1}_{\mathrm{PD}}(A)
. We often shorten this notation to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{crys}}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{crys}}
. When
\mathfrak{Z}=\Spf(R)
we shall shorten
(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
to
(R/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
, and similarly for other notation below.

The following example will appear frequently in the sequel (compare with Example
1.8
).

Example 2.16
.

Let
S
be a
p
-adically complete
W
-algebra with
S/p
semi-perfect. Then, if
S^{\flat}
denotes the perfection of
S/p
, there exists a universal
p
-adic pro-thickening
\theta\colon W(S^{\flat})\to S
over
W
(see
[
Fon94
, §1.2]
). Let
K=\ker(\theta)
, and let
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)
denote the
p
-adic PD-envelope of the pair
(W(S^{\flat}),K)
. Then,
\theta
extends to a
p
-adically continuous surjection
\theta\colon\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\to S
. The pair
(\theta\colon\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\to S,\gamma)
is a final object of
(S/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}

(see
[
Fon94
, Théorème 2.2.1]
).

There is a natural functor
u_{\mathrm{crys}}\colon(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{pr}}^{\mathrm{adic}}
, where
\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{pr}}^{\mathrm{adic}}
is the category of all adic formal
\mathfrak{Z}
-schemes equipped with the pr-topology as in
[
Lau18
, §7.1]
, by
u_{\mathrm{crys}}(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)\vcentcolon=\mathfrak{U}
. This functor is cocontinuous (as one can lift pr-covers along surjective closed embeddings), and so induces a morphism of topoi

(u_{\mathrm{crys},\ast},u_{\mathrm{crys}}^{-1})\colon\mathbf{Shv}((\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}})\to\mathbf{Shv}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{pr}}^{\mathrm{adic}}).

If
\mathfrak{Z}
is quasi-syntomic, then qrsp objects are a basis for
\mathfrak{Z}^{\mathrm{adic}}_{\mathrm{pr}}
(cf. the proof of
[
BMS19
, Lemma 4.28]
). So, one may prove the following much the same way as Proposition
1.11
.

Proposition 2.17
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{Z}
is quasi-syntomic. Then,
\{(\theta_{i}\colon\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S_{i})\to S_{i},\gamma_{i})\}
where
\{S_{i}\}
runs over
\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}
forms a basis of
\mathbf{Shv}((\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}})
.

The topos
\mathbf{Shv}((\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}})
is functorial. Namely, suppose that
f\colon\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{Z}
is an adic morphism of formal schemes lying over a morphism
W\to W
. There is then a morphism of topoi

(f_{\ast},f^{-1})\colon\mathbf{Shv}((\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}})\to\mathbf{Shv}((\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}),

and
f^{-1}
has a very concrete description, with
f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}=\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}^{\prime}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}
(see
[
BBM82
, 1.1.10]
).

The category of (iso)crystals

Let
\mathfrak{Z}\to\Spf(W)
be an adic morphism. Define the category of
finitely presented (resp. locally free) crystals
on
\mathfrak{Z}
, denoted
\mathbf{Crys}(\mathfrak{Z})
(resp.
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{crys}})
) to be the category of finitely presented (resp. locally free of finite rank) (in the sense of
[
SP
,
Tag 03DL
]
)
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-modules.
9
9

9

The forgetful functor
(\mathfrak{Z}/W(k))_{\mathrm{crys}}\to(\mathfrak{Z}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{crys}}
induces an equivalence of ringed topoi (see
[
BBM82
, 1.1.13]
). It is for this reason that we can be slightly imprecise with our notation for crystals.
A finitely presented (resp. locally free) crystal
\mathcal{E}
on
\mathfrak{Z}
is equivalent to the following data: for every
(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
a finitely presented (locally free of finite rank)
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}
-module
\mathcal{E}_{(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)}
and for every morphism
(f,g)\colon(i^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T}^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})\to(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
a morphism
g^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)}\to\mathcal{E}_{(i^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T}^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})}
such that the induced morphism
g^{\ast}\mathcal{E}_{(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)}\to\mathcal{E}_{(i^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T}^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})}
is an isomorphism (see
[
SP
,
Tag 07IT
]
). The association is as in
[
SP
,
Tag 07IN
]
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
set
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}\vcentcolon=(|\mathfrak{Z}|,\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}/p^{n+1})
(where by definition
\mathfrak{Z}_{\infty}=\mathfrak{Z}
). For
m\leqslant n
in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, denote by
\iota_{m,n}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{m}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{n}
the natural closed immersion. Then, one may check that
