(\iota_{m,n})_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{m}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}=\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}
, and by
[
dJ95a
, Lemma 2.1.4]
there are pairs of quasi-inverse equivalences

(2.3.2)

\displaystyle((\iota_{m,n})_{\ast},\iota_{m,n}^{\ast})\colon\mathbf{Crys}(\mathfrak{Z}_{m})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Crys}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}),

\displaystyle((\iota_{m,n})_{\ast},\iota_{m,n}^{\ast})\colon\mathbf{Vect}((\mathfrak{Z}_{m})_{\mathrm{crys}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}((\mathfrak{Z}_{n})_{\mathrm{crys}}).

For a crystal
\mathcal{E}
on
\mathfrak{Z}
, set
\mathcal{E}_{n}\vcentcolon=\iota_{n,\infty}^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
. For an object
(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
of
(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
, we have

\mathcal{E}(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)=\mathcal{E}_{n}(i_{n}\colon\mathfrak{U}_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)

as
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}(\mathfrak{T})
-modules. Here,
i_{n}
is the composition of the canonical closed embedding
\mathfrak{U}_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{U}
with
i
which, as
\gamma
is compatible with the PD structure on
W
, admits a unique extension (also denoted
\gamma
) to
\ker(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}\to(i_{n})_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{U}_{n}})=(p^{n},\ker(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{T}}\to i_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{U}}))
.

Remark 2.18
.

Note that while
\mathcal{J}_{(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}
is locally quasi-coherent (see
[
SP
,
Tag 07IS
]
), it does not satisfy the crystal condition. Nor is it true that
(\iota_{m,n})_{\ast}\mathcal{J}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{m}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}=\mathcal{J}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}
.

Finally, the category
\mathbf{Isoc}(\mathfrak{Z})
of
isocrystals
on
\mathfrak{Z}
has the same objects as
\mathbf{Crys}(\mathfrak{Z})
, denoted by
\mathcal{E}
or the formal symbol
\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
when clarity is needed, but with the following morphisms

\Hom(\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\mathcal{E}^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])\vcentcolon=\Gamma((\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}},\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}).

For any
m\leqslant n
in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
we again have an equivalence

((\iota_{m,n})_{\ast},\iota_{m,n}^{\ast})\colon\mathbf{Isoc}(\mathfrak{Z}_{m})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Isoc}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}).

We again denote by
\mathcal{E}_{n}
(or
\mathcal{E}_{n}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
), the pullback of
\mathcal{E}
(or
\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
) to
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}
.

The categories
\mathbf{Crys}(\mathfrak{Z})
,
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{crys}})
, and
\mathbf{Isoc}(\mathfrak{Z})
carry natural exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structures, and for
m\leqslant n
in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
the equivalences
(\iota_{m,n})_{\ast}
and
\iota_{m,n}^{\ast}
are bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalences.

F
-(iso)crystals

Let
\mathfrak{Z}\to\Spf(W)
be an adic morphism. The absolute Frobenius morphism
F_{\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}\colon\mathfrak{Z}_{0}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{0}
lies over the Frobenius
\phi\colon W\to W
, and so induces a morphism of ringed topoi

(\phi_{\ast},\phi^{\ast})\colon(\mathbf{Shv}((\mathfrak{Z}_{0}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}),\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}})\to(\mathbf{Shv}((\mathfrak{Z}_{0}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}),\mathcal{O}_{(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}}).

The category
\mathbf{Crys}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z})
(resp.
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z})
) of
F
-(iso)crystals
on
\mathfrak{Z}
has as objects pairs
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
(resp.
(\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\varphi_{\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]})
), where
\mathcal{E}
(resp.
\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
) is an (iso)crystal on
\mathfrak{Z}
and
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
(resp.
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}
) is a
Frobenius isomorphism

(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}_{0})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
of isocrystals, with morphisms those morphisms of (iso)crystals commuting with the Frobenii. Denote by
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{crys}})
the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Crys}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z})
of
F
-crystal whose underlying crystal is a vector bundle. Each of the categories
\mathbf{Crys}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z})
,
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{crys}})
, and
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z})
carry natural exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structures. For every
m\leqslant n
in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
the morphism
(\iota_{m,n})_{\ast}
induces quasi-inverse pairs
((\iota_{m,n})_{\ast},\iota_{m,n}^{\ast})

\mathbf{Crys}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{m})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Crys}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}),\,\,\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\mathfrak{Z}_{m})_{\mathrm{crys}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\mathfrak{Z}_{n})_{\mathrm{crys}}),\,\,\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{m})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}).

These are each bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalences.

Remark 2.19
.

Suppose that
(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
is an object of
(\mathfrak{Z}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
, and
\phi_{\mathfrak{T}}\colon\mathfrak{T}\to\mathfrak{T}
is a Frobenius lift compatible with the Frobenius map
\phi
on
W
. Fix a crystal
\mathcal{E}
on
\mathfrak{Z}
. From the morphism
(F_{\mathfrak{U}_{0}},\phi_{\mathfrak{T}})\colon(i_{0}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T}^{\prime},\gamma)\to(i_{0}\colon\mathfrak{U}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)
in
(\mathfrak{Z}_{0}/W)_{\mathrm{crys}}
, and the crystal property, there is an identification

\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}_{0}(i_{0}\colon\mathfrak{U}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)=\mathcal{E}(i_{0}\colon\mathfrak{U}_{0}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T}^{\prime},\gamma)\simeq\phi_{\mathfrak{T}}^{\ast}\mathcal{E}(i_{0}\colon\mathfrak{U}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma)=\phi_{\mathfrak{T}}^{\ast}\mathcal{E}(i\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{T},\gamma).

By
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}
) we denote
\mathfrak{T}
(resp.
\mathfrak{U}
) but with
W
-structure map (resp.
\mathfrak{Z}
-structure map) twisted by
\phi
(resp.
F_{\mathfrak{Z}_{0}}
), so the first equality holds by definition.

Base formal schemes and modules with connection

Let us now assume that
\mathfrak{Z}\to\Spf(W)
is a base formal
W
-scheme. Define
\mathbf{MIC}^{\mathrm{tqn}}(\mathfrak{Z})
to be the category of pairs
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
, where
\mathcal{V}
is a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
-module, and
\nabla\colon\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}}\Omega^{1}_{\mathfrak{Z}/W}
is an integrable topologically quasi-nilpotent connection (see
[
dJ95a
, Remark 2.2.4]
). Let
\mathbf{Vect}^{\nabla}(\mathfrak{Z})
denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{MIC}^{\mathrm{tqn}}(\mathfrak{Z})
of those pairs
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
where
\mathcal{V}
is a vector bundle. By
[
dJ95a
, Corollary 2.2.3]
, there are equivalences

\mathbf{Crys}(\mathfrak{Z})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{MIC}^{\mathrm{tqn}}(\mathfrak{Z}),\qquad\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathrm{crys}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\nabla}(\mathfrak{Z}),\qquad\mathcal{E}\mapsto(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Z}},\nabla_{\mathcal{E}}).

Here, for an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-module object
\mathcal{F}
, we denote by
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Z}}
-module given by associating to a Zariski open
\mathfrak{U}\subseteq\mathfrak{Z}
the value
\mathcal{F}(\id\colon\mathfrak{U}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{U},\gamma)
. These functors are bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalences, functorial in
\mathfrak{Z}
.

Filtered
F
-isocrystals

For a rigid
K
-space
Y
, denote by
\mathbf{Vect}^{\nabla}(Y)
the category of pairs
(V,\nabla_{V})
where
V
is a vector bundle on
Y
, and
\nabla_{V}\colon V\to V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\Omega^{1}_{Y/K}
is an integrable connection. Denote by
\mathbf{VectF}^{\nabla}(Y)
the category of triples
(V,\nabla_{V},\Fil^{\bullet}_{V})
where
(V,\nabla_{V})
is an object of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\nabla}(Y)
and
\Fil^{\bullet}_{V}
is a locally split filtration satisfying Griffiths transversality: for all
i\geqslant 0
the containment
\nabla_{V}(\Fil^{i}_{V})\subseteq\Fil^{i-1}_{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\Omega^{1}_{Y/K}
holds. The category
\mathbf{Vect}^{\nabla}(Y)
has an obvious exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structure, and we endow
\mathbf{VectF}^{\nabla}(Y)
with an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structure where

\Fil^{k}_{V_{1}\otimes V_{2}}=\sum_{i+j=k}\Fil^{i}_{V_{1}}\otimes\Fil^{j}_{V_{2}},

and an exact structure where a sequence is exact if for all
i
the sequence of vector bundles on
Y
given by the
i^{\text{th}}
-graded pieces is exact.

Suppose now that
\mathfrak{Z}\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is smooth with rigid generic fiber
Z
. In
[
Ogu84
, Remark 2.8.1 and Theorem 2.15]
, Ogus constructs an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor

\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{k})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\nabla}(Z),\qquad(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\mapsto(E,\nabla_{E}).

This functor possesses the following property. For any open
\mathfrak{V}\subseteq\mathfrak{Z}
, and any smooth model
\mathfrak{W}
of
\mathfrak{V}
over
W
, one has
(E,\nabla_{E})|_{\mathfrak{V}_{\eta}}
is isomorphic to
(\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{W}},\nabla_{\mathcal{F}})\otimes K
, where
\mathcal{F}=(\iota_{0,\infty})_{\ast}(\mathcal{E}|_{\mathfrak{W}_{0}})
, which is well-defined (i.e., doesn’t depend on
\mathcal{E}
within its isogeny class).

A
filtered
F
-isocrystal
on
\mathfrak{Z}
is a triple
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}},\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{E})
where
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is an object of
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{k})
and
\Fil^{\bullet}_{E}
is a locally split filtration on
E
satisfying Griffiths transversality. With the obvious notion of morphism, we denote by
\mathbf{IsocF}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z})
the category of filtered
F
-isocrystals on
\mathfrak{Z}
, which is seen to be identified with the fiber product
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{k})\times_{\mathbf{Vect}^{\nabla}(Z)}\mathbf{VectF}^{\nabla}(Z)
. We endow
\mathbf{IsocF}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z})
with the exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structure inherited from this decomposition and those structures on
\mathbf{Isoc}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{Z}_{k})
and
\mathbf{VectF}^{\nabla}(Z)
.

2.3.2.
de Rham local systems

Let
Y
be a smooth rigid
K
-space. Recall the following rings for an affinoid perfectoid space
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})\sim\varprojlim\Spa(R_{i},R_{i}^{+})
over
C
in
Y^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
:

•

\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(R^{+})
,

•

\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R^{+})\vcentcolon=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(S)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]^{\wedge}_{\xi_{0}}
,

•

\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(R^{+})\vcentcolon=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R^{+})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\xi_{0}}}]
,

•

\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R^{+})\vcentcolon=\varinjlim((R_{i}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{W}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(R^{+}))[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]^{\wedge}_{\ker(\theta)})
, given the
\ker(\theta)
-adic filtration, where
\theta\colon(R_{i}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{W(k)}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(S))[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to R
is the base extension of
\theta\colon\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(S)\to R^{+}
,

•

\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(R^{+})\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(R^{+})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}}]
with filtration

\Fil^{i}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{-j}\Fil^{i+j}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S)

which extend to sheaves on
Y^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. By
[
Sch13
, Corollary 6.13]
,
\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
carries a
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-horizontal integrable connection satisfying Griffiths transversality, extending to
\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
by base change.

An object
\mathbb{L}
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
is called
de Rham
if there exists an object
(V,\nabla_{V},\Fil^{\bullet}_{V})
of
\mathbf{VectF}^{\nabla}(Y)
, such that there exists an isomorphism of sheaves of modules over
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
:

c_{\mathrm{Sch}}\colon\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Fil^{0}\left(V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\right)^{\nabla=0},

where
\nabla\vcentcolon=\nabla_{V}\otimes\nabla_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}}}
and we endow
V\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
with the tensor product filtration. By
[
Sch13
, Theorem 7.6]
, the object
(V,\nabla_{V},\Fil^{\bullet}_{V})
is functorially associated to
\mathbb{L}
, and we denote it by
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})
(which we sometimes conflate with the underlying vector bundle). The category of
de Rham
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
local systems
is the essential image of the natural functor
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
and we set
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])\vcentcolon=D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})
(which is independent of the choice of
\mathbb{L}
).

Denote by
\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
the full subcategory of de Rham objects of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
, which is seen to be stable under tensor products and duals. The functor
D_{\mathrm{dR}}\colon\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)\to\mathbf{VectF}^{\nabla}(Y)
is an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor. If
Y=\Spa(K)
, these notations agree with those of Fontaine.

Fix an object
\omega
of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(Y)
and
y\colon\Spa(K^{\prime})\to Y
, for
K^{\prime}
a finite extension of
K
in
\overline{K}
. Let
\mathbf{VectF}(K^{\prime})
denote the category of finite-dimensional filtered
K^{\prime}
-vector spaces. Then, we have an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor

(\omega_{\mathrm{dR},y},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}})\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})\to\mathbf{VectF}(K^{\prime}),\quad\Lambda\mapsto(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\omega(\Lambda)),\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\omega(\Lambda))})_{y}.

Fix a conjugacy class
\bm{\mu}
of cocharacters of
G_{\overline{K}}
. We say
\omega
has
cocharacter
\bm{\mu}
if for all such
y
and all (equiv. for one)
\mu
in
\bm{\mu}
the following condition holds. There is an isomorphism
(\omega_{\mathrm{dR},y})_{\overline{K}}\simeq\omega_{\mathrm{triv}}
such that
(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}})_{\overline{K}}
is carried to
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mu}
where for a representation
\rho\colon\mathcal{G}\to\GL(\Lambda)
one has
\Fil^{r}_{\mu}=\bigoplus_{i\geqslant r}\Lambda_{\overline{K}}[i]
, where
\Lambda_{\overline{K}}[i]
is the
i
-weight space for the cocharacter
\rho\circ\mu
. The subcategory of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(Y)
of those
\omega
which have cocharacter
\bm{\mu}
is denoted by
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\bm{\mu}}(Y)
.

Finally, for a smooth
K
-scheme
Y
, denote by
\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
(resp.
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{dR}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\bm{\mu}}(Y)
) the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
(resp.
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
) consisting of those
\mathbb{L}
(resp.
\omega
) such that
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}
(resp.
\omega^{\mathrm{an}}
) belongs to
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(Y^{\mathrm{an}})
(resp.
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\bm{\mu}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(Y^{\mathrm{an}})
).

2.3.3.
Crystalline local systems

As in
[
TT19
, §2A]
, recall the following rings for an affinoid perfectoid
C
-space
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})\sim\varprojlim\Spa(R_{i},R_{i}^{+})
in
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
:

•

\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+})
, filtered by
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{PD}}
,

•

\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(R^{+})\vcentcolon=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
filtered by
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{PD}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
,

•

\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\vcentcolon=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(R^{+})\vcentcolon=\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(S)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}}]
, with filtration given by

\Fil^{i}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{-j}\Fil^{i+j}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(R,R^{+}).

These rings, and their filtrations, extend to sheaves on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. The Frobenius on
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}
extends uniquely to
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}
as
p
is Frobenius invariant, and further extends uniquely to
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
with
\phi(\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}})=\nicefrac{{1}}{{pt}}
(see
[
TT19
, §2C]
). In particular, for any object
\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
the sheaf
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
carries a natural Frobenius and filtration.

The Faltings formulation

For an object
(\mathcal{E},\varphi,\Fil^{\bullet}_{E})
of
\mathbf{IsocF}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X})
and an affinoid perfectoid space
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
over
C
in
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, which uniquely extends to a map
\Spf(R^{+})\to\mathfrak{X}
(e.g. those factorizing through
\Spf(A)_{\eta}
for an affine open
\Spf(A)\subseteq\mathfrak{X}
), we have that
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)
is a pro-infinitesimal PD thickening of
R^{+}
, so we have the associated
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(R,R^{+})
-module

\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})(R,R^{+})\vcentcolon=\mathcal{E}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(R,R^{+})\twoheadrightarrow R^{+})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}}].

As in
[
GR24
, §2.3]
, this extends to a sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
and inherits a Frobenius morphism
\varphi\colon\phi^{\ast}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})
and a filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E})}
induced from
(\mathcal{E},\varphi,\Fil^{\bullet}_{E})
.

As in
[
Fal89
, p. 67]
, call an object
\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)
(resp. object
\mathbb{L}
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
)
crystalline relative to

\mathfrak{X}
if there exists an object
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{E})
of
\mathbf{IsocF}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X})
and an isomorphism of
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}
-modules

c_{\mathrm{Fal}}\colon\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{E}),

compatible with Frobenius and filtration (resp.
\mathbb{L}
is crystalline). Denote by
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X)
(resp.
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X)
), suppressing
\mathfrak{X}
from the notation, the full subcategory of crystalline
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-local (resp.
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local) systems. By the arguments in
[
GR24
, Corollary 2.35]
, the filtered
F
-isocrystal
\mathcal{E}
is functorial in
\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
. We say that
(\mathcal{E},\varphi,\Fil^{\bullet}_{E})
and
\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
are
associated
, and write
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})
for
\mathcal{E}
. We futher define
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})\vcentcolon=D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
for a crystalline
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
\mathbb{L}
. As explained in
[
TT19
, Proposition 3.22]
and
[
GR24
, Corollary 2.37]
, any crystalline local system
\mathbb{L}
on
X
is de Rham, and if
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})=(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{E})
then
D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})
is equal to
(E,\nabla_{E},\Fil^{\bullet}_{E})
(cf. §
2.3.1
).

Let
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid
C
-space
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
in
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, which uniquely extends to a map
\Spf(R^{+})\to\mathfrak{X}
. We consider the isomorphism

\theta^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}\colon D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\to R^{+})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(S)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+})(S)

from the paragraph right after
[
TT19
, Proposition 3.21]
. Put
\theta_{\mathrm{crys}}\vcentcolon=\theta_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}}]
.
Let
\theta^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}
denote the scalar extension of
\theta^{+}_{\mathrm{crys}}
along
\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}^{+}(S)\to\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S)
, and

(2.3.3)

\theta^{+,\nabla}_{\mathrm{dR}}\colon D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\to R^{+})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+})(S)^{\nabla=0}

be the map induced on the spaces of horizontal sections. Put
\theta^{\nabla}_{\mathrm{dR}}\vcentcolon=\theta^{+,\nabla}_{\mathrm{dR}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}}]
. Since, for a crystalline local system
\mathbb{L}
on
X
, the isomorphism
c_{\mathrm{Sch}}
is given as the scalar extension of the composition
\theta_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ c_{\mathrm{Fal}}
as explained in the proof of
[
GR24
, Corollary 2.37]
, we obtain the following lemma, which is used in the proof of Theorem
3.21
.

Lemma 2.20
.

Assume that
\mathbb{L}|_{S}
is constant.
Then the diagram

\textstyle{(\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\mathrm{Fal}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{c_{\mathrm{Sch}}\otimes 1}
\textstyle{D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)\to R^{+})\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(S)}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\nabla}}
\textstyle{(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})(S)^{\nabla=0}}

commutes.

For a conjugacy class
\bm{\mu}
of cocharacters of
G_{\overline{K}}
denote by
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\bm{\mu}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X)
those
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local systems which lie in both
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X)
and
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\bm{\mu}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(X)
.

The Brinon formulation

Fix a base
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
A=A_{0}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}
, with formal framing
t\colon T_{d}\to A_{0}
. Consider the following rings:

•

\mathcal{O}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
is the
p
-adically completed PD envelope of the map
\theta\colon A_{0}\otimes_{W}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\check{A})\to\check{A}
, filtered by
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{PD}}
,

•

\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}}]
filtered by

\Fil^{r}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})=\sum_{n\geqslant-r}t^{-n}\Fil^{n+r}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A}).

The tensor product Frobenius
\phi
on
A_{0}\otimes_{W}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
uniquely extends to
\mathcal{O}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
and we can extend the Frobenius to
\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
with
\phi(\nicefrac{{1}}{{t}})=\nicefrac{{1}}{{pt}}
. There is a natural connection

\nabla\colon\mathcal{O}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})\to\mathcal{O}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})\otimes_{A_{0}}\widehat{\Omega}^{1}_{A_{0}/W},

with
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
and
\phi
horizontal (see
[
Kim15
, Proposition 4.3]
), which extends to
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
by base change. There is a natural action of
\Gamma_{A}
on
\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
and the tautological morphism
A_{0}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})^{\Gamma_{A}}
is an isomorphism (see
[
Bri08
, Proposition 6.2.9]
).

For a finite-dimensional continuous
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-representation
\rho\colon\Gamma_{A}\to\GL_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(V)
, write

D_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)\vcentcolon=(\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}V)^{\Gamma_{A}},

which is an
A_{0}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
-module. There is a natural morphism of
\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})
-modules

\alpha_{\mathrm{crys}}\colon\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})\otimes_{A_{0}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}D_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)\to\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\check{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}V

and following
[
Bri08
]
we say
\rho
(or
V
) is
crystalline
if it is an isomorphism. This notion is independent of the choice of
A_{0}
(see
[
Bri08
, Proposition 8.3.5]
). A finite-rank free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation
\Lambda
of
\Gamma_{A}
is
crystalline
if
\Lambda[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is, in which case we write
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\Lambda)\vcentcolon=D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\Lambda[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
.

Denote by
\mathbf{Rep}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\Gamma_{A})
(resp.
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(\Gamma_{A})
) the category of crystalline
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-representations (resp.
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations) endowed with the evident structure of an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category. If
V
is an object of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\Gamma_{A})
then
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(V)
has the structure of an object of
\mathbf{IsocF}^{\varphi}(A)
, and
[
Bri08
, Théorème 8.5.1]
defines a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor

D_{\mathrm{crys}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\Gamma_{A})\to\mathbf{IsocF}^{\varphi}(A),

which is a bi-exact equivalence onto its image, functorial in
A
.

Let
\Sigma
be either
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. If
\{\Spf(A)\}
is a small open cover of
\mathfrak{X}
, then an object
\mathbb{L}
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\Sigma}(X)
is crystalline if and only if the
\Sigma
-representation
V_{A}
associated to
\mathbb{L}|_{\Spf(A)_{\eta}}
is crystalline for all
A
(see
[
DLMS24
, Proposition A.10]
). In this case we have that
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathbb{L})|_{\Spf(A)_{\eta}}
agrees with
D_{\mathrm{crys}}(V_{A})
for all
A
. Thus,

D_{\mathrm{crys}}\colon\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X)\to\mathbf{IsocF}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}),

is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor which is a bi-exact equivalent onto its image. Also,
\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\Sigma}(X)
is closed under duals, tensor products, direct sums, and subquotients (see
[
Bri08
, Théorème 8.4.2]
).

Proposition 2.21
.

For an object
\omega
of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\Sigma}(X)
,
\omega
factorizes through
\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\Sigma}(X)
if and only if
\omega(V_{0})
is crystalline for some faithful
\Sigma
-representation
V_{0}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the if condition. Moreover, as
\omega
is crystalline if and only if
\omega[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is, we may suppose that
\Sigma=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. By
[
Del82
, Proposition 3.1 (a)]
every object
V
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G)
occurs as a subquotient of
\bigoplus_{i}(V_{0})^{\otimes m_{i}}\oplus(V_{0}^{\vee})^{\otimes n_{i}}
for some finite list of integers
m_{i}
and
n_{i}
. As
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X)
is closed under duals, tensor products, direct sums, and subquotients, the claim follows.
∎

2.3.4.
Crystalline
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local systems

Consider the
étale realization functor

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X),

defined to be the composition

\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})\xrightarrow{\,T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\,}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X),

where the first functor is obtained by patching together the pullbacks
\Spec(A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]^{\wedge}_{p})\to U(A,I)
.

Theorem 2.22

(
[
GR24
]
(cf.
[
DLMS24
]
))
.

The functor
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
induces an equivalence

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X).

We devote the rest of this subsection to proving the following claim.

Proposition 2.23
.

The functor

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)

is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence. In particular,
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
induces an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X).

As the base extension functor
\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}]^{\wedge}_{p})
is exact, as an exact sequence of vector bundles is universally exact (in the sense of
[
SP
,
Tag 058I
]
), exactness of
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
follows from Proposition
2.13
. So, we have reduced ourselves to showing that
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}
is exact. But, combining Proposition
1.11
and Proposition
1.20
we are reduced to showing the following.

Proposition 2.24
.

If
R
is a small
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra, then the following composition is exact:

\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(\Gamma_{R})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Spa(R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]))\xrightarrow{T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(R_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{eval.}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an}}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}),(\tilde{\xi})).

Define

\displaystyle\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{[p^{\flat}]^{p}}}{{p}}]^{\wedge}_{p}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]

\displaystyle\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{p}}{{[p^{\flat}]^{p}}}]^{\wedge}_{[p^{\flat}]^{p}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{[p^{\flat}]^{p}}}]

\displaystyle\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{B}_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}\vcentcolon=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{[p^{\flat}]^{p}}}{{p}},\nicefrac{{p}}{{[p^{\flat}]^{p}}}]^{\wedge}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p[p^{\flat}]^{p}}}],

where the final completion is either the
p
-adic or
[p^{\flat}]^{p}
-adic completion.
10
10

10

As in
[
SW20
, §12.2]
, denote by
\mathcal{Y}
the analytic locus
\Spa(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))^{\mathrm{an}}
. This is an analytic adic space equipped with a surjective continuous map
\kappa\colon|\mathcal{Y}|\to[0,\infty]
. For
[a,b]\subseteq[0,\infty]
with rational (possibly infinite) endpoints, set
\mathcal{Y}_{[a,b]}\vcentcolon=\kappa^{-1}([a,b])^{\circ}
, which is a rational open subset of
\Spa(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))
. When comparing references it is useful to observe that for each
[a,b]
in
\{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty],[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\}
there is a natural isomorphism
\mathrm{B}_{[a,b]}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathcal{Y}_{[a,b]})
.
We denote by
\widetilde{\varphi}
the following composition

\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]=\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R})[\tfrac{\xi^{n}}{n!}]^{\wedge}_{p}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R})[\tfrac{\tilde{\xi}^{n}}{n!}]^{\wedge}_{p}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\hookrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{[p^{\flat}]^{p}]}}{{p}}]^{\wedge}_{p}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]=B_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]},

which we use to view
\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}
as an
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
-algebra.

There is a natural pullback functor

\mathbf{Vect}\left(\Spec(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))-V(p,[p^{\flat}]^{p})\right)\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]})\times_{\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}})}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}),

as the natural maps
\Spec(\mathrm{B}_{[a,b]})\to\Spec(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))
for
[a,b]\in\{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]\}
factorize through
\Spec(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))-V(p,[p^{\flat}]^{p})
, and are equalized when composed with
\Spec(\mathrm{B}_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}})\to\Spec(\mathrm{B}_{[a,b]})
. For a vector bundle
M
on
\Spec(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))-V(p,[p^{\flat}]^{p})
we denote by
M_{[a,b]}
, for
[a,b]\in\{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]\}
, the induced vector bundle on
\mathrm{B}_{[a,b]}
.

Lemma 2.25

(cf.
[
Ked20
, Theorem 3.8]
)
.

The functor

\mathbf{Vect}\left(\Spec(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))-V(p,[p^{\flat}]^{p})\right)\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]})\times_{\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}})}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]})

is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence.

Proof.

By the proof of
[
Ked20
, Theorem 3.8]
, it remains to prove this functor is bi-exact. The exactness follows as an exact sequence of vector bundles is universally exact. To prove that the quasi-inverse is exact, from acyclicity of vector bundles on sheafy affinoid adic spaces and
[
Ked20
, Proposition 3.2]
as a whole, we deduce exactness of the quasi-inverse to the functor in
[
Ked20
, Proposition 3.2 (a)]
, which we apply to (defaulting to the notation in
[
Ked20
, Definition 3.5]
)
A_{1}\to B_{1}\oplus B_{2}^{\prime}
and
A_{2}\to B_{1}^{\prime}\oplus B_{2}
to get the desired exactness.
∎

Remark 2.26
.

The proof of this lemma implies exactness of Kedlaya’s equivalence between vector bundles on
\Spec(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))-V\left(p,[p^{\flat}]^{p}\right)
and those on
\Spa(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))_{\mathrm{an}}
(cf. Footnote
10
), which seems well-known. Though the adic space perspective could clarify the following proof of Lemma
2.24
, we have chosen to avoid it for the sake of brevity.

We now recall some structural properties of the functor
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}
.

Lemma 2.27
.

For an object
\Lambda
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(\Gamma_{R})
, set
\mathcal{M}\vcentcolon=T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\Lambda)
and
M\vcentcolon=\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}),(\tilde{\xi}))
.

(1)

There is a natural isomorphism of
\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}
-modules

M_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{E}=D_{\mathrm{crys}}(\Lambda)
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

(2.3.4)

M_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{E}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}].

Proof.

In the proof of
[
GR24
, Theorem 4.8]
, the prismatic crystal
\mathcal{M}
is described as the vector bundle
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{R}}
on
\Spec(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))-V\left(p,[p^{\flat}]^{p}\right)
constructed in
[
GR24
, Theorem 4.15]
, together with a descent datum on
\widetilde{R}\hat{\otimes}_{R}\widetilde{R}
. In particular,
M
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{R}}
. In the proof of
[
GR24
, Theorem 4.15]
, the module
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{R}}
is constructed by gluing the
\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}
-module
\mathcal{M}_{3,\widetilde{R}}
constructed in
[
GR24
, Proposition 4.11]
and the
\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}
-module
\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}
via the equivalence in Lemma
2.25
. In particular, assertion (1) follows by definition. To prove assertion (2), note that
\mathcal{M}_{3,\widetilde{R}}
is obtained by the Beauville–Laszlo gluing of the
\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}]
-module

\mathcal{E}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}]

and a certain
(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]})_{\tilde{\xi}}^{\wedge}
-module (specifically the submodule
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}^{0}_{E}
of the
(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]})_{\tilde{\xi}}^{\wedge}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}]
-module
\mathcal{E}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widetilde{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],\tilde{\varphi}}(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]})_{\tilde{\xi}}^{\wedge}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}]

). So, (2) again follows by definition.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.24
.

By Lemma
2.25
we are reduced to the assertions that the functors

\displaystyle\mathbf{Rep}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma_{R})\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}),\qquad

\displaystyle\Lambda\mapsto T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\Lambda)(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]},

\displaystyle\mathbf{Rep}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma_{R})\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}),\qquad

\displaystyle\Lambda\mapsto T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\Lambda)(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]},

are exact. The first functor being exact follows immediately from the first assertion of Lemma
2.27
and the fact that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]}
is flat. As Beauville–Laszlo gluing is exact, to prove that the second functor is exact, it suffices to show that the functors

(2.3.5)

\mathbf{Rep}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma_{R})\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}]),\qquad\Lambda\mapsto T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\Lambda)(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\tilde{\xi}}}],

and

(2.3.6)

\mathbf{Rep}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma_{R})\to\mathbf{Vect}((\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]})^{\wedge}_{\tilde{\xi}}),\qquad\Lambda\mapsto\left[T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\Lambda)(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(\widetilde{R}))_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]}\right]_{\tilde{\xi}}^{\wedge},

are exact. The functor in (
2.3.5
) being exact follows from the second assertion of Lemma
2.27
as
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
is exact. To see that the functor in (
2.3.6
) is exact, we observe that there is an identification
(\mathrm{B}_{[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]})_{\tilde{\xi}}^{\wedge}=(\mathrm{B}_{[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}},\infty]})_{\tilde{\xi}}^{\wedge}
, and thus we are again reduced to the first assertion of Lemma
2.27
.
∎

2.4.
\mathcal{G}
-objects in the category of prismatically good reduction local systems

We now wish to extend some of the results of the last subsection to the case of prismatic
F
-crystals. For the remainder of this subsection, we assume that
\mathfrak{X}\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is smooth and
\mathcal{G}
is reductive.

Definition 2.28
.

The category
\mathbf{Loc}^{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
of
prismatically good reduction

\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems on
X
(relative to
\mathfrak{X}
) is the full exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-subcategory of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(X)
consisting of those
\mathbb{L}
with
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\mathbb{L})
a prismatic
F
-crystal on
\mathfrak{X}
.

If
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, then every crystalline
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system has prismatically good reduction (cf.
[
GR24
, Proposition 3.8]
), but for higher-dimensional
\mathfrak{X}
this ceases to be the case (cf.
[
DLMS24
, Example 3.36]
). There is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

(2.4.1)

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Loc}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X).

This is exact as
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
is, and so induces a functor

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X).

That said, the quasi-inverse to the functor in (
2.4.1
) is not exact even for
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K}
) as its evaluation at the Breuil–Kisin prism is the functor
\mathfrak{M}
from
[
Kis06
]
(see
[
BS23
, Remark 7.11]
), which is known to not be exact (e.g. see
[
Liu18
, Example 4.1.4]
).

But, despite the functor in (
2.4.1
) not being bi-exact, we still have the following.

Theorem 2.29
.

The functor

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

From Proposition
2.23
, it is clear that this functor is fully faithful. To show that it is essentially surjective, fix
\omega
in
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}^{\mathrm{crys}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
such that
\omega(\Lambda_{0})
has prismatically good reduction. Write
(\mathcal{E}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{0}})
for the associated object of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
and let
(\mathcal{V}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{V}_{0}})
denotes its image in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
. As the functor
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
is fully faithful, we see that the tensors

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\omega(\mathds{T}_{0}))\subseteq\Hom\left((\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{an}},\phi),(\mathcal{V}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{V}_{0}})^{\otimes}\right)

obtained from Proposition
2.23
uniquely lift to a set of tensors
\mathds{T}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}\subseteq(\mathcal{E}_{0},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}_{0}})^{\otimes}
. Set

\mathcal{Q}_{\omega}\vcentcolon=\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathds{T}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\right),

with the Frobenius structure inherited from
(\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathds{T}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
. This is a pseudo-torsor for
\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
on
\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
.

Proposition 2.30
.

The pseudo-torsor
\mathcal{Q}_{\omega}
is a torsor.

Proof.

For any small affine open subset
\Spf(R)
of
\mathfrak{X}
, set

(M_{R},\mathds{T}_{R}):=(\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathds{T}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E)),\quad\mathcal{Q}_{\omega,R}\vcentcolon=\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathfrak{S}_{R},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(M_{R},\mathds{T}_{R})\right),

considered as a pseudo-torsor for
\mathcal{G}
on
\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R})_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. By Corollary
1.16
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{Q}_{\omega,R}
is a torsor for all such
R
. But, the restriction of
\mathcal{Q}_{\omega,R}
to
U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))
is identified with

\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},E)},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(\mathcal{V}_{0},T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\omega(\mathds{T}_{0})))|_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))}\right).

Proposition
2.23
implies that
\mathcal{Q}_{\omega,R}|_{U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},E)}
is a torsor. As the height of
(p,E)\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{R}
is
2
and
M_{R}
is a vector bundle,
\mathcal{Q}_{\omega,R}
is a torsor by Proposition
A.28
or Remark
A.29
.
∎

Let
\nu
be the object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
associated to
\mathcal{Q}_{\omega}
by Proposition
1.29
. We claim that
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\nu

is isomorphic to
\omega
. But, by Proposition
1.29
it suffices to observe that, by setup, both
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\nu
and
\omega
have the value
(\omega(\Lambda_{0}),\omega(\mathds{T}_{0}))
when evaluated on
(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
.
∎

As a byproduct of the above proof and Theorem
A.14
(which implies every faithful representation can be upgraded to a tensor package) we obtain an analogue of Proposition
2.21
.

Corollary 2.31
.

Let
\Lambda
be a faithful representation of
\mathcal{G}
. Then, an object
\omega
of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
belongs to
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathfrak{X})
if and only if
\omega(\Lambda)
is an object of
\mathbf{Loc}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
.

Proposition
1.29
and Theorem
2.29
yield an equivalence
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}(X)
which we also denote by
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
(or
T_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
), which is compatible in
\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathcal{G}
in the obvious way.

2.5.
Complementary results about base
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras

While the proof of Theorem
2.29
was built on the work of
[
GR24
]
, Proposition
2.30
works more generally, using
[
Kis10
, Proposition 1.3.4]
and the results of
[
DLMS24
]
. This could be potentially useful in other contexts (e.g., it provides an alternative method to prove some results in
[
IKY23
]
).

Let
R
be a (formally framed) base
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra. In
[
DLMS24
, §4.4]
,
11
11

11

While in loc. cit. the authors only construct this functor for non-negative Hodge–Tate weights, this definition can be easily extended using Breuil–Kisin twists.
there is constructed a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor

\mathfrak{M}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(\Gamma_{R})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E)).

Let
j
denote the inclusion
U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))\hookrightarrow\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R})
. We say that a representation
\Lambda
in
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(\Gamma_{R})
has
prismatically good reduction
if
j_{\ast}\mathfrak{M}(\Lambda)
is a vector bundle on
\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R})
.

Let us suppose that
\Lambda_{0}
carries the structure of an object of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{crys}}(\Gamma_{R})
. Denote
(\mathfrak{M}(\Lambda_{0}),\mathfrak{M}(T_{0}))
by
(M^{\mathrm{an}},\mathds{T}^{\mathrm{an}})
, and denote the global sections of
j_{\ast}(\mathfrak{M}(\Lambda_{0}),\mathfrak{M}(T_{0}))
by
(M,\mathds{T})
.

Proposition 2.32
.

Consider the following sheaf on
U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))_{\mathrm{fpqc}}
:

\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{an}}=\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(M^{\mathrm{an}},\mathds{T}^{\mathrm{an}})\right).

Set
\mathcal{Q}\vcentcolon=j_{\ast}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{an}}
. Then
\mathcal{Q}
is a reflexive pseudo-torsor and if
\mathcal{G}
is reductive, then

\mathcal{Q}=\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathfrak{S}_{R},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(M,\mathds{T})\right),

and is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor on
\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R})_{\mathrm{fppf}}
if and only if
\Lambda_{0}
has prismatically good reduction.

Proof.

Given Proposition
A.27
and Proposition
A.28
, the latter claims will follow if we can show that
\mathcal{Q}
is a reflexive pseudo-torsor. As
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{an}}
is clearly affine and finite type over
U(\mathfrak{S}_{R},E)
, we further deduce from Proposition
A.24
that it suffices to prove
\mathcal{Q}
is a torsor after pulled back to all codimension
1
points of
\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R})
.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}
) denote the
p
-adic completion of the localization
R_{(p)}
(resp.
(R_{0})_{(p)}
). Note that
\mathcal{O}_{L}=\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}
is a base
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra, and write
(\mathfrak{S}_{L},(E))
for its Breuil–Kisin prism. Furthermore, let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
denote the
p
-adic completion of
\mathfrak{S}_{R}[u^{-1}]
. As the morphism
\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{L})\sqcup\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})\to\Spec(\mathfrak{S}_{R})
is flat (see
[
SP
,
Tag 00MB
]
) and its image contains all codimension
1
points, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{Q}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor on pullback to
\mathfrak{S}_{L}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
.

To prove the first claim, note that the perfection
\varinjlim_{\phi}\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}
is a discrete valuation ring that is faithfully flat over
\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}
. In particular, its
p
-adic completion
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}_{0}}
is faithfully flat over
\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}}
. The ring
\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}_{L^{\prime}_{0}}\otimes_{W}\mathcal{O}_{K}
is a complete discrete valuation ring with perfect residue field, and, with notation as above,
\mathfrak{S}_{L^{\prime}}
is faithfully flat over
\mathfrak{S}_{L}
. Thus, it suffices to show
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathfrak{S}_{L^{\prime}}}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor. Recall though that
M\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}_{R}}\mathfrak{S}_{L^{\prime}}
is canonically identified with the (classical) Breuil–Kisin module associated to
\rho
restricted to the absolute Galois group of
L^{\prime}
as in
[
Kis06
]
(see
[
DLMS24
, Lemma 4.18 and Proposition 4.26]
). Thus, from
[
Kis10
, Proposition 1.3.4]
we see that
\mathcal{Q}(\mathfrak{S}_{L^{\prime}})
is non-empty.

For the second claim, observe that (cf.
[
DLMS24
, Proposition 4.26]
) there is an isomorphism

M\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}_{R}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E}},

in
\mathbf{Mod}^{\varphi}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E}})
, functorial in
\Lambda_{0}
, where
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{ur}}
is the
p
-adic completion of a colimit of finite étale extensions
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathcal{E}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
with compatible extension of
\phi
(see
[
DLMS24
, §2.3]
). From this functorial isomorphism, we see that
\mathcal{Q}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{ur}})
is non-empty, and so
\mathcal{Q}|_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor.
∎

3.
Shtukas and analytic prismatic
F
-crystals

In this final section we recall the notion of a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka on a (formal) scheme and discuss the relationship to (analytic) prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure. Any undefined notation and conventions concerning shtukas, their accompanying adic spaces (e.g. the spaces
\mathcal{Y}_{I}(S)
), and
v
-sheaves are as in
[
PR24
, §2]
.

Notation 3.1
.

Fix the following notation:

•

k
to be a perfect extension of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
,

•

W\vcentcolon=W(k)
and
E_{0}\vcentcolon=\mathrm{Frac}(W)
,

•

E
is a totally ramified finite extension of
E_{0}
with uniformizer
\pi
,

•

\overline{E}
is an algebraic closure of
E
which induces an algebraic closure
\overline{k}
of
k
,

•

C
denote the
p
-adic completion of
\overline{E}
,

•

\breve{W}\vcentcolon=W(\overline{k})
,

•

\mathcal{G}
is a parahoric group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme with generic fiber denoted
G
,

•

\bm{\mu}
is a conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G_{\overline{E}}
with field of definition
E
over
E_{0}
.

Remark 3.2
.

The assumption that
\mathcal{G}
is parahoric is needed only when bounding the relative position of two lattices by
\mathbb{\mu}
. Otherwise, everything in this section works under the assumption that
\mathcal{G}
is a smooth affine group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme with connected fibers.

3.1.
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas

In this subsection we recall the definition of a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over a (formal)-scheme. We fix notation as in Notation
3.1
.

3.1.1.
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas over
v
-sheaves

Denote by
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
the category of affinoid perfectoid spaces
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
with
R^{+}
a
k
-algebra, endowed with the
v
-topology. For any
v
-sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
we let
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{F}}
denote the slice site over
\mathcal{F}
. In other words, an object of
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{F}}
is a pair
(S,\alpha)
where
S
is an object of
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
and
\alpha\colon S\to\mathcal{F}
is a morphism, a morphism
(S_{1},\alpha_{1})\to(S_{2},\alpha_{2})
is a map
f\colon S_{1}\to S
such that
\alpha_{2}\circ f=\alpha_{1}
, and such a map is a
v
-cover if
f
is.

Recall that if
\mathscr{X}
is a pre-adic space over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, then
\mathscr{X}^{\lozenge}
is the
v
-sheaf on
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
associating to
S
the set of pairs
(S^{\sharp},f)
where
S^{\sharp}
is an untilt of
S
and
f\colon S^{\sharp}\to\mathscr{X}
is a morphism of adic spaces. This construction is functorial in
\mathscr{X}
. We shorten the slice site
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathscr{X}^{\lozenge}}
to
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathscr{X}}
and further to
\mathbf{Perf}_{A}
if
\mathscr{X}=\Spa(A)
. We observe that objects of
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathscr{X}}
can be interpreted as pairs
(S^{\sharp},f)
where
S^{\sharp}
is a perfectoid space over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, and
f\colon S^{\sharp}\to\mathscr{X}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-morphism, and we often use this interpretation without comment.

Let us denote by

\mathbf{Sht}\colon\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\to\mathbf{Grpd},\quad\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}\colon\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\to\mathbf{Grpd},\quad\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}_{\bm{\mu}}\colon\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\to\mathbf{Grpd}

the
v
-stacks associating to
(S,S^{\sharp},\beta)
the groupoid of shtukas over
S
with a single leg at
S^{\sharp}
, the groupoid of
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas over
S
with a single leg at
S^{\sharp}
, and the groupoid of
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas over
S
with a single leg at
S^{\sharp}
bounded by
\bm{\mu}
, respectively, as in
[
PR24
, Definition 2.4.3]
.

Definition 3.3

(
[
PR24
, Definition 2.3.1]
)
.

For a
v
-sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
and
\mathscr{S}
an element of
\{\mathbf{Sht},\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht},\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}_{\bm{\mu}}\}
, we define
\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{F})
to be the groupoid of maps of
v
-stacks
\mathcal{F}\to\mathscr{S}
.

Equivalently, each
\mathscr{S}
as in Definition
3.3
defines a natural stack
p_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathscr{S}\to\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{F}}
and the groupoid
\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{F})
may be identified with the groupoid of Cartesian sections of
p_{\mathcal{F}}
(cf.
[
SP
,
Tag 07IV
]
). In other words, an object of
\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{F})
as a functorial rule associating to each
(S,\alpha)
in
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{F}}
an object of
\mathscr{S}(S,\alpha^{\prime})
where
\alpha^{\prime}
is the composition
S\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\text{structure}}\Spd(\mathcal{O}_{E})
. We treat these interpretations as interchangeable below.

Remark 3.4
.

Let
\mathscr{S}
be a
v
-stack on
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
. Then, one can naturally form the left Kan extension
\mathscr{S}\colon\mathbf{PSh}(\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}})\to\mathbf{Grpd}
, since
\mathbf{Grpd}
is
2
-complete. Concretely,

\mathscr{S}(\mathcal{F})\vcentcolon=\twolim_{S\to\mathcal{F}}\mathscr{S}(S).

The above notions of shtuka-like objects on
v
-sheaves are a special case of this construction, as
\mathscr{S}
commutes with
2
-limits, and
\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\lim_{S\to\mathcal{F}}S
(e.g., see
[
SGA4-1
, Exposé I, 3.4.0]
). This is related to the interpretation as Cartesian sections via the Grothendieck construction.

We call an object of
\mathbf{Sht}(\mathcal{F})
,
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}(\mathcal{F})
,
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}_{\bm{\mu}}(\mathcal{F})
a
shtuka over
\mathcal{F}
, a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over
\mathcal{F}
, and a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over
\mathcal{F}
bounded by
\mu
, respectively. These groupoids are evidently
2
-functorial in
\mathcal{F}
, and in fact form stacks on
\mathbf{Sh}(\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}})
.

We often write an object of any of these shtuka-like groupoids over
\mathcal{F}
as
(\mathscr{P},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}})
. For an object
(S,\alpha)
of
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
, and an element of
\mathcal{F}(S,\alpha)
(i.e., a morphism
(S,\alpha)\to\mathcal{F}
or, equivalently, an element of
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{F}}
), we may pull back
(\mathscr{P},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}})
to an object over
(S,\alpha)
. We denote this pullback by evaluation, i.e., by
(\mathscr{P},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}})(S,\alpha)
.

Finally, let us observe that
\mathbf{Sht}(\mathcal{F})
is actually an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category. Indeed, for each map
(S,\alpha)\to\mathcal{F}
we get a map
(S,\alpha^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
which corresponds to a triple
(S,S^{\sharp},\beta)
. The category
\Sht(S,S^{\sharp},\beta)
is an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category in the usual way as vector bundles on
S\dot{\times}\Spa(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
with meromorphic Frobenius away from a fixed locus (i.e.,
S^{\sharp}\subseteq S\dot{\times}\Spa(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
). As the morphisms
\Sht(S,S^{\sharp},\beta)\to\Sht(S_{1},S_{1}^{\sharp},\beta_{1})
are exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functors for a morphism
(S_{1},S_{1}^{\sharp},\beta_{1})\to(S,S^{\sharp},\beta)
(exact because exact sequences of vector bundles are universally exact), we see that we may endow
\mathbf{Sht}(\mathcal{F})
with the structure of an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category by passing to the
2
-limit. It’s clear that this structure is functorial in
\mathcal{F}
.

Remark 3.5
.

It is evident (cf.
[
SW20
, Appendix to Lecture 19]
) that, as the notation suggests,
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}(\mathcal{F})
is the category of
\mathcal{G}
-objects in
\Sht(\mathcal{F})
. In particular, there is a natural identification

\GL_{n}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}(\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Sht}_{n}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq\mathbf{Sht}(\mathcal{F}),

where the target is the full subgroupoid of shtukas over
\mathcal{F}
of rank
n
. We shall make these identifications freely in the sequel.

3.1.2.
Some
v
-sheaves associated to (formal) schemes

We will be mostly interested in studying shtuka-like objects over
v
-sheaves which are obtained from (formal) schemes. In particular, we recall the following notational definitions.

•

If
\mathscr{X}
is a locally of finite type
E
-scheme we shorten
(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{an}})^{\lozenge}
to
\mathscr{X}^{\lozenge}
and shorten
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{an}}}
to
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathscr{X}}
.

•

If
\mathscr{X}
is a locally formally of finite type formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme we shorten
(\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{\lozenge}
to
\mathscr{X}^{\lozenge}
and shorten
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathscr{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}}
to
\mathbf{Perf}_{\mathscr{X}}
.

•

If
\mathscr{X}
is a separated locally of finite type
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme then we define

(3.1.1)

\mathscr{X}^{\lozenge/}=\widehat{\mathscr{X}}^{\lozenge}\sqcup_{(\widehat{\mathscr{X}}^{\lozenge})_{E}}(\mathscr{X}_{E})^{\lozenge}.

In (
3.1.1
) the morphism
(\widehat{\mathscr{X}}^{\lozenge})_{E}\to(\mathscr{X}_{E})^{\lozenge}
is the open embedding obtained via the composition

(\widehat{\mathscr{X}}^{\lozenge})_{E}=(\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{E})^{\lozenge}\xrightarrow{j_{\mathscr{X}}^{\lozenge}}(\mathscr{X}_{E})^{\lozenge},

where
j_{\mathscr{X}}\colon\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{E}\to\mathscr{X}_{E}^{\mathrm{an}}
is the open embedding from
[
Hub96
, §1.9]
.

3.1.3.
Shtukas over (formal) schemes

With the definitions from §
3.1.2
, we may import the definitions from §
3.1.1
.

Definition 3.6
.

Let
\mathscr{S}
be an element of
\{\mathbf{Sht},\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht},\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}_{\bm{\mu}}\}
:

•

if
\mathscr{X}
is a locally of finite type
E
-scheme or a locally of finite type formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme, we define
\mathscr{S}(\mathscr{X})\vcentcolon=\mathscr{S}(\mathscr{X}^{\lozenge})
,

•

if
\mathscr{X}
is a separated finite type
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme, we define
\mathscr{S}(\mathscr{X})\vcentcolon=\mathscr{S}(\mathscr{X}^{\lozenge/})
.

To help contextualize this second definition, we make the following further definition.

Definition 3.7
.

The category
\mathbf{Tri}(\mathcal{O}_{E})
of
gluing triples
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
has

•

objects of the form
(X,\mathfrak{X},j)
with
X
a separated locally of finite type
E
-scheme,
\mathfrak{X}
a separated locally of finite type flat formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme, and
j\colon\mathfrak{X}_{\eta}\to X^{\mathrm{an}}
an open embedding,

•

morphisms
(f,g)\colon(X_{1},\mathfrak{X}_{1},j_{1})\to(X_{2},\mathfrak{X}_{2},j_{2})
with
f\colon X_{1}\to X_{2}
a morphism of
E
-schemes and
g\colon\mathfrak{X}_{1}\to\mathfrak{X}_{2}
a morphism of formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-schemes, such that
f^{\mathrm{an}}\circ j_{1}=j_{2}\circ g_{\eta}
.

If
\mathscr{X}
is a separated locally of finite type flat
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme, one may functorially associate a gluing triple
(\mathscr{X}_{E},\widehat{\mathscr{X}},j_{\mathscr{X}})
. This in particular gives a functor

(3.1.2)

\mathsf{t}\colon\left\{\begin{matrix}\text{Locally of finite type}\\
\text{separated flat }\mathcal{O}_{E}\text{-schemes}\end{matrix}\right\}\to\mathbf{Tri}(\mathcal{O}_{E}),\qquad\mathscr{X}\mapsto\mathsf{t}(\mathscr{X})=(\mathscr{X}_{E},\widehat{\mathscr{X}},j_{\mathscr{X}}).

We use
j_{\mathscr{X}}
to consider
\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{\eta}
as an open adic subspace of
\mathscr{X}_{E}^{\mathrm{an}}
without comment in the sequel.

Proposition 3.8
.

The functor
\mathsf{t}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

By a standard devissage, we reduce ourselves to the following. Suppose that
A
is a finite type flat
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-algebra. Then, the preimage of
\widehat{A}
under
A_{E}\to\widehat{A}_{E}
is
A
. Indeed, writing an element of
A_{E}
as
\tfrac{a}{\pi^{k}}
with
a
in
A
, by assumption
a
maps into
\pi^{k}\widehat{A}
and thus
a
lies in the preimage of
\pi^{k}\widehat{A}
which is the kernel of
A\to\widehat{A}\to\widehat{A}/\pi^{k}\widehat{A}=A/\pi^{k}A
and so
\pi^{k}A
. Thus
\tfrac{a}{\pi^{k}}
is in
A
as desired.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

See
[
AY24
]
for a much stronger version of Proposition
3.8
.

Now, fix a gluing triple
(X,\mathfrak{X},j)
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. For
\mathscr{S}
any element of
\{\mathbf{Sht},\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht},\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}_{\bm{\mu}}\}
we denote by
\mathscr{S}(X,\mathfrak{X},j)
the category of triples
(s_{X},s_{\mathfrak{X}},\gamma)
where
s_{X}
and
s_{\mathfrak{X}}
are objects of
\mathscr{S}(X)
and
\mathscr{S}(\mathfrak{X})
respectively, and
\gamma\colon j^{\ast}(s_{X})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}s_{\mathfrak{X}}|_{\mathfrak{X}_{\eta}}
is an isomorphism in
\mathscr{S}(\mathfrak{X}_{\eta})
, with the obvious notion of morphisms. Then, for a separated finite type
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme
\mathscr{X}
there are natural equivalences

(3.1.3)

\mathsf{t}\colon\mathscr{S}(\mathscr{X})\to\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{t}(\mathscr{X}))

functorial in
\mathscr{X}
. Therefore, we shall often not differentiate between
\mathscr{S}(\mathscr{X})
and
\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{t}(\mathscr{X}))
below.

3.2.
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas and local systems

We now recall and elaborate on the relationship between
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas and
\mathcal{G}
-objects in de Rham local systems as in
[
PR24
]
.

3.2.1.
Shtukas and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-pairs

We begin by elaborating on the relationship between shtukas on
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-pairs. Throughout the following we fix
X
to be a locally Noetherian adic space over
E
.

Theorem 3.10

(
[
PR24
, §2.5.1–§2.5.2]
)
.

There is an equivalence of categories

(3.2.1)

\Phi_{X}\colon\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}(X)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\left\{(\mathbb{P},H)\colon\begin{aligned} (1)&\,\,\mathbb{P}\emph{isanobjectof}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(X),\\
(2)&\,\,\emph{a}\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\emph{-equivariantmap}H\colon\mathbb{P}^{\lozenge}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Spd}(E)}.\end{aligned}\right\}.

Let us elaborate on the notation on the right-hand side of (
3.2.1
):

•

\mathbb{P}^{\lozenge}=\varprojlim(\mathbb{P}/K_{n})^{\lozenge}
is the diamond associated to
\mathbb{P}
as in §
2.1.6
,

•

\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Spd}(E)}
is the
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-Grassmannian associated to
G
as in
[
FS21
, §III.3]
,

•

and
H
is a morphism of
v
-sheaves over
\mathrm{Spd}(E)
.

The morphisms in the target category in (
3.2.1
) are the obvious ones.

Remark 3.11
.

Although in
[
PR24
]
, the target category is defined with
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(X^{\lozenge})
instead of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(X)
, these groupoids are equivalent (see Remark
2.10
).

Remark 3.12
.

The target category of the functor
\Phi_{X}
fits into the Tannakian framework in the following sense. Let
S^{\sharp}
be an affinoid perfectoid space over
E
and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\mathchar 45\mathbf{Lat}_{\mathcal{G}}(S^{\sharp})
denote the target category. We let
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\mathchar 45\mathbf{Lat}^{\mathrm{vect}}(S^{\sharp})
denote the groupoid of triples
(\mathbb{L},M,\psi\colon\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}M\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp}))
where
\mathbb{L}
is a finite free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
S^{\sharp}
(or equivalently on the tilt
S
),
M
is a finite projective
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})
-module, and
\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})
is the finite projective
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})
-module constructed as in the beginning of
[
SW20
, §22.3]
. Then with the notation in Definition
A.16
, there is an equivalence

\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\mathchar 45\mathbf{Lat}_{\mathcal{G}}(S^{\sharp})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}\mathchar 45\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\mathchar 45\mathbf{Lat}^{\mathrm{vect}}(S^{\sharp}).

In fact,
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\mathchar 45\mathbf{Lat}_{\mathcal{G}}(S^{\sharp})
is identified with the groupoid of triples
(\mathbb{P},\mathcal{Q},\psi\colon\mathbb{P}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}G_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{Q}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp}))
where
\mathbb{P}
is an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}(S^{\sharp})=\mathbf{Tors}_{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}(S)
,
\mathcal{Q}
is a
G
-torsor on
\Spec(\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp}))
, and
\mathbb{P}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}G_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})}
denotes the
G
-torsor on
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})
constructed via
[
SW20
, Theorem 22.5.2]
(cf.
[
FS21
, §III.3]
).

To explain the definition of the functor
\Phi_{X}
, begin by fixing an object of
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
of
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
as well as an untilt
S^{\sharp}=\Spa(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp+})
over
E
with
(\xi)\vcentcolon=\ker(\theta\colon W(R^{+})\to R^{\sharp+})
. Also, for a closed subset
Z
of a topological space
Y
and a sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
Y
, we write
\Gamma^{\dagger}(Z,\mathcal{F})
as shorthand for
\varinjlim_{Z\subseteq U}\mathcal{F}(U)
.

As in
[
KL15
, Definitions 4.2.2 and 5.1.1]
, consider the
integral Robba ring (over
S
)

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{S}\vcentcolon=\varinjlim_{r\to 0^{+}}\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Y}_{[0,r]}(S))=\Gamma^{\dagger}(S,\mathcal{O}_{S\dot{\times}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

For this last object, we are considering
S
as a closed Cartier divisor of
S\dot{\times}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in the usual way (see
[
FS21
, Proposition II.1.4]
). We observe that
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{S}
carries a natural Frobenius morphism, compatible with that on
S\dot{\times}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. As
S^{\sharp}
does not intersect
S
we see that we have a functor

\mathbf{Sht}_{n}(S^{\sharp})\to\mathbf{Mod}^{\varphi}(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{S}),\qquad(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})\mapsto(\Gamma^{\dagger}(S,\mathcal{P}),\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}),

where the target is the category of étale
\varphi
-modules over
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{S}
(see
[
KL15
, Definition 6.1.1]
).

Denote by
\mathbf{Sht}_{n,\mathrm{free}}(S^{\sharp})
the full subgroupoid of
\mathbf{Sht}_{n}(S^{\sharp})
consisting of shtukas
(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
with the property that the associated object of
\mathbf{Mod}^{\varphi}(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{S})
is free (which is called ‘trivial’ in loc. cit.). On the other hand, let
{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-}\mathbf{Pair}_{n}(S^{\sharp})
be the groupoid of pairs
(T,\Xi)
, where
T
a finite free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module of rank
n
and,
\Xi
is a
{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})
-lattice of
T\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})
, with the obvious notion of (iso)morphisms.

There is a natural morphism of groupoids

\Phi_{S^{\sharp}}\colon\mathbf{Sht}_{\mathrm{n,free}}(S^{\sharp})\to{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-}\mathbf{Pair}_{n}(S^{\sharp}),\qquad(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})\mapsto(T_{\mathcal{P}},\Xi_{\mathcal{P}}).

Here
T_{\mathcal{P}}\vcentcolon=\Gamma^{\dagger}(S,\mathcal{P})^{\varphi=1}
, which is a free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module of rank
n
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})}
denote
\Gamma(\mathcal{Y}_{[0,1]},\mathcal{P})^{\wedge}_{\xi}
, and set
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\xi}}]
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

(3.2.2)

T_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{B}}^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})}\,\,(=\Gamma^{\dagger}(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{P})^{\wedge}_{\xi}),

obtained by extending an element of
T_{\mathcal{P}}
, which is a priori an element of
\Gamma(\mathcal{Y}_{[0,r]}(S),\mathcal{P})
for some
r>0
, to
\Gamma(\mathcal{Y}_{[0,1]},\mathcal{P})
via
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
(cf.
[
SW20
, Corollary 12.4.1]
). Set
\Xi_{\mathcal{P}}
to be the
{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})
-lattice in
T_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})
corresponding under (
3.2.2
) to
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}((\phi^{*}\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})})
where

\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}\colon(\phi^{*}\mathcal{P})_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})}\to\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})},

is the map induced by the Frobenius structure of
\mathcal{P}
.

If an object
(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
of
\mathbf{Sht}_{n,\mathrm{free}}(S^{\sharp})
is obtained by restriction from an object
(M,\varphi_{M})
of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(W(R^{+}),(\xi))
then we can describe
\Phi_{S^{\sharp}}(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
more concretely. Namely, we have the equality
T_{\mathcal{P}}=(M\otimes_{W(R^{+})}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{S}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\varphi=1}
, and
\Xi_{\mathcal{P}}
corresponds under (
3.2.2
) to the image of the
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})
-lattice
\phi^{\ast}M\otimes_{W(R^{+})}{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})
under

\varphi_{M}\otimes 1\colon\phi^{\ast}M\otimes_{W(R^{+})}{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})\to M\otimes_{W(R^{+})}{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp}).

In any case, we have the following result, which can be proven exactly in the same way as
[
SW20
, Proposition 12.4.6]
, as mentioned in the proof of
[
PR24
, Proposition 2.5.1]
.

Lemma 3.13

(cf.
[
SW20
, Proposition 12.4.6]
and
[
PR24
, Proposition 2.5.1]
)
.

The functor

\Phi_{S^{\sharp}}\colon\mathbf{Sht}_{n,\mathrm{free}}(S^{\sharp})\to{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\text{-}\mathbf{Pair}_{n}(S^{\sharp}),\qquad(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})\mapsto(T_{\mathcal{P}},\Xi_{\mathcal{P}})

is an equivalence.

When
\mathcal{G}=\GL_{n,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, the equivalence
\Phi_{X}
is then obtained from the observations that

(a)

\Phi_{S^{\sharp}}
defines a functor on
\mathbf{Perf}_{X}
,

(b)

the source and target are the global sections of the stackification of
\mathbf{Sht}_{n,\mathrm{free}}
and
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\mathchar 45\mathbf{Pair}_{n}
for the pro-étale topology, respectively, where here we consider these objects as prestacks on
\mathbf{Perf}_{X}
.
12
12

12

The fact that every object of
\mathbf{Sht}_{n}(S^{\sharp})
is pro-étale locally in
\mathbf{Sht}_{n,\text{free}}(S^{\sharp})
follows from
[
KL15
, Theorem 8.5.3]
as any local system can be trivialized pro-étale locally.

The case for general
\mathcal{G}
is then obtained by applying the Tannakian formalism (see Remarks
3.5
and
3.12
).

Let
\Gr_{G,\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
and
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
be as in
[
SW20
, Definition 19.2.2]
. Observe that if
\bm{\mu}
is minuscule, then
\Gr_{G,\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}=\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
. The following lemma is just an unraveling of the definitions.

Lemma 3.14
.

If
(\mathscr{P},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}})
is an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}(X)
, and
\Phi_{X}(\mathscr{P},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}})=(\mathbb{P},H)
, then
(\mathscr{P},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}})
is an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}_{\bm{\mu}^{-1}}(X)
if and only if
H
factorizes through
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
.

Proof.

Let us begin by observing that as
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
is a closed subdiamond of
\Gr_{G,\Spd(E)}
(see
[
SW20
, Proposition 19.2.3]
). Thus,
H
factorizes through
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
if and only if it does so at the level of points. Moreover, as
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
is closed in
\Gr_{G,\Spd(E)}
it is partially proper (see
[
SW20
, Lemma 19.1.4]
), and so we further see that
H
factorizes through
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}
if and only if it does so for points of the form
S^{\sharp}=\Spa(C^{\sharp},C^{\sharp\circ})
with
C
an algebraically closed perfectoid field. Tracing through the definitions, this means that for trivializations
G_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C^{\sharp+})}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C^{\sharp+})}
and
G_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C^{\sharp+})}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\phi^{*}\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C^{\sharp+})}
, that the relative position of
\mathscr{P}
and
\varphi_{\mathscr{P}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathscr{P})
defines an element of
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu},\Spd(E)}(C^{\sharp},C^{\sharp\circ})
. On the other hand, by definition,
(\mathscr{P},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}})
lies in
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}_{\bm{\mu}^{-1}}(X)
if and only if for all such
S^{\sharp}
and all such trivializations, the relative position of
\varphi_{\mathscr{P}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{P})
and
\mathscr{P}
defines an element of
\Gr_{G,\leqslant\bm{\mu}^{-1},\Spd(E)}(C^{\sharp},C^{\sharp\circ})
. These are clearly equivalent.
∎

3.2.2.
Shtukas associated to de Rham local systems

Let
X
be a smooth rigid
E
-space. In
[
PR24
, §2.6.1–§2.6.2]
there is constructed a functor

U_{\mathrm{sht}}\colon\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\to\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Sht}(X),

which we now recall.

Thanks to Theorem
3.10
, it suffices to construct a functor

\Phi_{X}\circ U_{\mathrm{sht}}\colon\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(X)\to\left\{(\mathbb{P},H)\colon\begin{aligned} (1)&\,\,\mathbb{P}\text{ is an object of }\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(X),\\
(2)&\,\,\text{a }\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-equivariant map }H\colon\mathbb{P}^{\lozenge}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Spd}(E)}.\end{aligned}\right\}.

Further, by employing the Tannakian formalism (see Remark
3.12
), we may further assume that
\mathcal{G}=\GL_{n,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. In this case, an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(X)
is nothing but a rank
n
de Rham local system
\mathbb{L}
on
X
. Then one defines
\Phi_{X}(U_{\mathrm{sht}}(\mathbb{L}))
to be
(\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{L}},H_{\mathbb{L}})
. Here,
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{L}}=\underline{\Isom}(\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{n},\mathbb{L})
is the
\underline{\GL_{n}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor as in §
2.1.2
. Recall, as in §
2.3.2
, there is a canonical
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-equivariant isomorphism

c_{\mathrm{Sch}}\colon\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Fil^{0}(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})^{\nabla=0}.

This induces a canonical
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
-equivariant isomorphism

c_{\mathrm{Sch}}\otimes 1\colon\mathbb{L}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}})^{\nabla=0}.

So, from an isomorphism
a\colon\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{n}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbb{L}_{S^{\sharp}}
, where
S^{\sharp}
is an untilt over
X
of some
S
in
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
, we obtain an isomorphism of
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})
-modules

(c_{\mathrm{Sch}}\otimes 1)\circ a\colon\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{n}(S^{\sharp})\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S^{\sharp})}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})(S^{\sharp})\otimes_{S^{\sharp}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp}))^{\nabla=0}.

We then set

H_{\mathbb{L}}(a)\vcentcolon=((c_{\mathrm{Sch}}\otimes 1)\circ a)^{-1}((D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L})(S^{\sharp})\otimes_{S^{\sharp}}\mathcal{O}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp}))^{\nabla=0}),

a
\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(S^{\sharp})
-lattice in
\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{n}(S^{\sharp})\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S^{\sharp})}\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}(S^{\sharp})
.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
\omega
be an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(X)
and write
\Phi_{X}(U_{\mathrm{sht}}(\omega))=(\mathbb{P},H)
. Then, we have that
\omega
belongs to
